| D65 | 2026-09-23 | **Templates: the infrastructure, and the one rule that makes it safe to apply them.** Phase 5 batch T1 — the **Shapes | Templates** sidebar switcher, the empty-canvas start panel, the sample-strip button, `File ▸ New from template…`, the browser, the apply flow, the `src/templates/` format with lazy per-category loading, and three starters. **The rule everything else hangs off: nothing from a template may keep its ids.** A template is applied repeatedly, sometimes into a document that already holds a copy of itself, so reusing ids collides nodes, edges, ports and whole child diagrams — and the collision reads as data corruption rather than a bug. Every id is re-issued and every reference rewritten through the same map: `childDiagramId`, port `innerNodeId`, and both ends of a boundary-crossing edge including its `portId`. `instantiate()` is pure for the same reason: the template module is a shared cached object, so mutating it would poison the second application. **Applying is a single `doc` replacement, which is what makes it one undo entry** — child diagrams included — rather than a sequence of addPage/addNode calls that would need four Ctrl+Z presses to back out. **Replacing a page also deletes the child diagrams that page owned**, or every apply would leave an invisible pile of orphans behind. **A template is just a `DeepDrawDoc`**, so it gets the same `validateDoc()`, the same renderer, and the same nesting as a user file — nothing about templates is a special case at the model layer. Thumbnails are the existing nested-block miniature renderer pointed at the template's own page: no hand-made images to drift out of date. `usesNesting` is derived, never hand-set, because a flag is a second source of truth. **The spec's hard rule is enforced by test, not review**: every `shapeRef` must resolve in the registry, and every port a nested template ships must already be mapped — an unmapped one renders as an amber "waiting to be linked" warning, and a template that opens looking unfinished is worse than no template. **The stencil audit before building found no gaps**, so T1 stayed infrastructure plus content | User asked for T1 as one PR, starting with the audit. Verified live through all four entry points, the nested starter drilling into its child with a ghost link, and one Ctrl+Z restoring 2 diagrams → 1 and 5 nodes → 0 with an empty undo stack. Two things fixed on sight: "More Shapes…" was leaking into the Templates tab, and the start panel's non-capturing layer was checked by asking what is actually under the cursor beside the card (the React Flow pane, not the layer). 706 unit / 86 E2E green |
| D66 | 2026-09-24 | **Software & UML templates, and the rule that the library has to be able to draw them first.** Phase 5 batch T2 — eight templates, led by the **C4 model as three real nesting levels** (Context → Container → Component). C4 is *defined* as a zoom: each level is the previous one's box opened up, which every other tool renders as three separate diagrams and asks the reader to hold the relationship in their head. Here the Internet Banking System **is** the container diagram and the API Application **is** the component diagram — one document, opened by clicking. This is the strongest case for nested blocks in the catalogue. **The audit came first and it found real gaps**, unlike T1's: UML's meaning lives in its line ends, and the hollow triangle (inherits), filled diamond (composition) and hollow diamond (aggregation) simply did not exist in the app. **A template must never use notation the user cannot otherwise produce by hand**, so those went into the Style panel before a single template was written — a unit test now asserts every line end in every Software & UML template is one the Style panel offers. **Arrowheads got a single source of truth** (`src/canvas/arrowheads.ts`): the canvas, the SVG export and the Style panel had three separate notions of the vocabulary, so a shape added to one would silently fail to export or fail to appear. Two bugs the consolidation exposed: a hollow head drawn with `fill="none"` lets the edge line show straight through its middle (so a head declares `fill: "stroke" | "background" | "none"`), and heads were hardcoded to ink, so a red edge exported with a black arrowhead (fixed with `context-stroke`). Also added the **C4 stencil section** (6 shapes), four missing UML shapes, and search keywords on all 14 existing UML stencils — a stencil nobody can find is a stencil that does not exist. | User asked for T2 as one PR, starting with the audit. All 10 new shapes verified rendering in the browser and `c4` search returning the section. One pre-existing E2E broke and the break was real, not flaky: the empty-canvas start panel's tile grid grew when C4 joined the featured six, so an unrelated test's fixed "click the bare canvas" coordinate landed on a template tile — that test now finds a point that is actually the React Flow pane instead of trusting a constant. 735 unit / 88 E2E green |
| D67 | 2026-09-24 | **An export must be the same renderer as the canvas, not a second one that agrees.** User reported that pasting into Word/PowerPoint gave straight lines where the canvas showed curves, a blank box where the Nested Block was, serif text, and clipped edge labels. **Root cause: `file/svgExport.ts` was a parallel reimplementation of the canvas, and its own header comment claimed otherwise** — "reuses the same pure path builders the live edge renderer uses" was true only for the branch almost no edge takes. `DiagramEdge` branches on waypoint count: with waypoints it uses the shared polyline builders, with **none** — the normal case — it uses React Flow's `getBezierPath`/`getSmoothStepPath`. The exporter always used the polyline builders, and `buildSmoothPolyline` returns a straight line under three points. Two points is what a waypoint-free edge has, so **every curved edge in the app exported as a straight one**, which a probe confirmed: nine curved edges, zero curve commands. **The lesson is about where the seam was, not about the bug.** Two renderers that are supposed to agree will not, because nothing forces them to and nobody reads both in one sitting. The fix is therefore structural: `canvas/edgeRouting.ts` now owns handle anchors and path building and **both** callers use it; `nesting/miniatureSvg.ts` owns the Nested Block thumbnail and both callers use it; the exporter resolves stencil defaults through the same `getStencilDefaultStyle` the canvas uses. **Anything a canvas component and the exporter could disagree about belongs in a shared module** — that rule is now written at the top of both files. This mattered widely because every consumer routes through `diagramToSvg`: PNG, `.deepdraw.png`, SVG, PDF, print, PPTX, DOCX, copy-with-live-link and the Slide Zoom thumbnails were all wrong in the same four ways. **The font exemption was self-defeating**: `fontTokens.test.ts` exempted svgExport on the reasoning that naming IBM Plex would make exports fall back on machines without it — but leaving the system stack *guaranteed* the fallback, and edge labels named no family at all and came out serif. Embedding the woff2 as base64 `@font-face` removes the reason for the exemption; the two weights are loaded on demand so they stay out of the main bundle. **Bugs nobody reported, found by reading the two side by side**: icons exported as outlined blobs (the exporter hardcoded white/ink/2 instead of asking `getStencilDefaultStyle`, which gives a pictogram a flat silhouette and an outline shape a 1px line), and multi-line labels ran together because a single `<text>` ignores `
| D68 | 2026-09-24 | **Data templates, and the audit paying for itself twice.** Phase 5 batch T3 — ER (Chen), ER (crow's foot), ★⧉ **Levelled DFD (Yourdon/DeMarco)**, ⧉ Levelled DFD (Gane & Sarson), and a data pipeline. **The levelled DFD is the flagship and the best-fitting use of nesting yet**: a DFD is *defined* by levelling — the context diagram's single process is "exploded" into Level 1 — and every textbook draws the two as separate figures and asks the reader to hold the link in their head. Here the process **is** the Level 1 diagram. Better still, DFD's **balancing rule** (a child's external flows must match its parent process's flows) stops being a thing to check by eye: the boundary flows are ports, so the structure enforces it, and a test asserts every boundary edge goes through a port that maps to a real Level 1 process. **The audit found four gaps and two things nothing else would have caught.** Gaps: the five crow's-foot cardinality marks, an entire missing DFD section, no crow's-foot entity table, and zero keywords on all 10 entity-relation stencils. The two it caught that review would not: `arrowheads.test.ts` used `"crows-foot"` as its stand-in for an *unknown* arrowhead kind, so adding it would have turned that assertion silently false; and **`ArrowheadDef` structurally could not express the notation** — one `path` and one `fill`, when "zero or many" is a stroked fork *plus* an opaque ring. It now carries `parts: {d, fill}[]`, which is a real generalisation the notation demanded, not a speculative one. **Geometry of this kind can only be judged by looking at it**: the first crow's foot put the qualifier bar inside the fork's prongs, so "one or many" read as one smudge — caught on screen, not by a test, and fixed by shortening the fork to leave room behind it. **A canvas bug this batch exposed, which had already shipped**: the label element had no `white-space` rule, so a flex box collapsed every authored `
| D69 | 2026-09-24 | **Cloud templates, and two bugs that only a picture could show.** Phase 5 batch T4 — ★⧉ **Azure hub-and-spoke landing zone**, network topology, AWS three-tier web app, GCP basic web app, and ⧉ a Kubernetes cluster. Hub-and-spoke is the clearest architectural case for nesting after C4: a landing-zone diagram has two jobs that fight each other — show the shared platform, and show what each workload team runs — so drawn flat either the hub drowns in workload detail or the spokes become empty boxes labelled "Spoke 1". Each spoke is its own diagram, peered to the hub through a mapped port. **The audit found the icon sets sufficient but surfaced a renderer bug that would have wrecked the flagship**: `miniatureSvg.ts` drew every vendor icon as a blank tile, on the stated reasoning that a raw vendor `<svg>` with its own viewBox "can't be re-anchored by wrapping it in a `<g>`". True, and beside the point — a *nested* `<svg>` with x/y/width/height establishes a new viewport the inner viewBox scales into, which is exactly what ShapeNode and svgExport already do for these icons. Left alone, every spoke thumbnail would have been a grid of empty rectangles. **The second bug was invisible to every test and obvious on screen**: a boundary box is a node, React Flow draws nodes above the edge layer, and the boxes were filled white — so the Azure hub's icons appeared connected to nothing. Boundaries are now outlines, which is also how AWS, Azure and Google draw them in their own reference architectures, and how the vendors draw them was the tell that the fill was never needed. **Templates have to be architecturally honest, not just tidy**: the AWS load balancer was first drawn inside Availability Zone A, which says the whole app fails when that zone does, and S3 sat inside the VPC, which is not where S3 is. Both are now asserted by test rather than by eye. **Two constraints accepted rather than worked around**: Kubernetes uses generic shapes and no vendor artwork, per the spec's own condition, because no openly licensed icon set is in the repo and adding one is a provenance task of its own; and GCP's published set simply has no per-product Load Balancing, CDN or DNS icon — we already ship it in full — so the front door uses the official networking *category* icon rather than something scraped. | User asked to merge #43 and start T4 with the audit. All five templates applied and drilled into on screen; the miniature fix verified against real Azure icons before any template depended on it. Featured is now exactly six, the spec's cap. Three E2E failures in this batch were all my own harness, not the product — a start-panel button for a template that is not featured, clicks landing off-screen on diagrams wider than the viewport, and reading the document before an async template apply had landed. 796 unit / 101 E2E green |
| D70 | 2026-09-24 | **Business templates, a BPMN section, and `align` turning out to have never worked.** Phase 5 batch T5 — ⧉ org chart, cross-functional process, BPMN (basic), fishbone, SWOT, business model canvas, customer journey map and milestone timeline. **The audit found the largest gap of any batch**: no BPMN section at all and no swimlane containers, so 13 shapes went in first. Pool and lane live in the BPMN section rather than one of their own, because a swimlane *is* a pool with lanes — that is where the idea comes from — and their keywords carry "swimlane" and "cross-functional" so nobody has to know that. **Each BPMN event is its own stencil even though the notation's difference is only border weight**: a reader picking from a palette should not have to know that an end event is a circle you thicken, and a template relying on it would look like a bug the first time someone reset the line width. **The bug this batch exposed had been there since the beginning**: `RichLabel.align` did nothing on the canvas. The label element is a flex box with `justify-content: center`, which positions the text *block*; `text-align` only positions lines within that block, so for a single-line label it had no effect at all — while the SVG export honoured `align` via `text-anchor`. A BPMN lane's name is what exposed it: set left so it would sit in the name band, it rendered in the middle of the diagram on top of the first task. **Third instance of canvas/export drift** after D67 and D69, and the first where the canvas was the side that was wrong. **A percentage-width band does not survive a stretched shape**: `svgBody` is authored in a 100-unit box, so a 14% name band on a 1094px lane came out 150px wide with the name stranded beside it; narrowed to 6%. | User asked to merge #44 and start T5 with the audit. All eight templates applied and inspected on screen; the lane-name and multi-line-label fixes are both guarded by E2E checked to fail without them. Keywords added to `flowchart` (0/12) and `business` (20 missing) because those carry domain vocabulary; `general` and `basic` deliberately left to the Inbox, being primitives already findable by their own names. **A T5 template broke two T3 tests, and the cause is worth remembering**: those tests waited on a page-wide `getByText("CUSTOMER")`, which Playwright matches case-insensitively — so the new "Customer journey map" card in the sidebar satisfied the wait instantly, before the template under test had applied. Loose page-wide text matchers get more fragile with every template added; both are now scoped to the canvas. 823 unit / 105 E2E green |
| D71 | 2026-09-24 | **Fill opacity, and the end of Phase 5.** Batch T6 — Venn two-set and three-set, tree diagram, decision tree, concept map and sitemap. **The smallest audit gap of any batch, and the spec had called it**: "requires fill opacity on ellipses; verify the Style panel supports it, add it if not". It did not. The panel had whole-node `opacity`, which is a different thing: it fades the shape, its border *and* its label together, so a Venn built on it has washed-out outlines and grey text. **Fill opacity is fill-only, and that distinction is the entire feature** — the overlaps have to blend while the outlines and labels stay crisp. Added as a `fillOpacity` style key and wired through all four renderers in one go (`StencilGlyph`, `ShapeNode`, `svgExport`, `miniatureSvg`) rather than the two that would have made the Venns work, because the canvas and the export have now drifted apart three times (D67, D69, D70) and the cheapest moment to keep them together is when the key is added. It gets its own Style panel slider, per the templates spec's standing rule that a template may never use something the app cannot otherwise produce. **Two layout faults found by looking, not by testing**: the three-set Venn's title overlapped a set label, and the concept map ran "Habit → Churn" straight through the Trust box — which in a concept map is not untidiness but a false claim, since a line touching a node reads as a relationship. **Phase 5 is complete**: T1 through T6, **35 templates** across six categories (process 2, software 8, data 5, architecture 5, business 8, thinking 7), six featured. | User asked to merge #45 and start T6 with the audit. All six templates applied and inspected on screen; the fill-opacity wiring verified against two real overlapping ellipses before any template relied on it, and guarded by an E2E checked to fail without the canvas half of it. 844 unit / 108 E2E green |
| D72 | 2026-09-25 | **Stop finding drift by screenshot: compare the two renderers directly, every template, every run.** The canvas and the export had disagreed three times (D67, D69, D70), each caught only because someone looked at a picture, each having passed unit tests that examined one renderer at a time. `tests/e2e/m14-render-parity.spec.ts` renders all 35 templates and every diagram nested inside them through both paths, lines elements up by model id (the exporter gained an opt-in `annotateIds`; real exports are byte-identical), and compares what a reader sees — label text and line breaks, alignment, font, colour, fill and fill opacity, strokes, markers, dashes, vendor icons, the Nested Block's title, miniature and ports — plus one geometric rule: every exported coordinate is the canvas coordinate plus a single translation, so any edge whose start or end disagrees by more than 0.75px has had its handle resolved differently. A kitchen-sink document covers what no template uses. **Its first run found four real bugs, one of them severe.** (1) **The canvas had drawn no arrowheads at all since T2 (#40).** React Flow takes a string `markerEnd` as an id and wraps it in `url('#…')` itself; it was handed `markerUrl`'s already-wrapped value, producing `url('#url(#dd-arrow-classic)')`, which points at nothing. Every export drew arrowheads correctly, so the two disagreed on every directed edge in the product for five merged PRs. The T2 E2E meant to guard this checked `.includes("dd-arrow-triangle")` — a broken reference *contains* the name — so it passed throughout; it and its T3 twin now resolve the reference. **Lesson: assert that a reference resolves, never that a string contains a name.** (2) **A port attachment was stored twice** — `end.portId` and a `"port:<id>"` handle string — and different code paths updated different copies. `applyTemplate` re-issued `portId` but not the string, so a template's port edge vanished; `removePort` cleared `portId` but not the string, so the edge vanished from the canvas while staying in the file and in every export (its comment claimed it "degrades to a plain node-level connection"); `connect` and `reconnectEdge` set `portId` to an auto-created port while leaving the string as the dropped-on side, so the line ended at the side's midpoint away from its own dot. | D79 | 2026-09-28 | **Inserting your own image: scoped.** Full scope in `docs/DeepDraw_ImageInsert_Scope_20260928_v1.0.md`. **An image is a shape, not a new node type:** a `ShapeNode` with `shapeRef: "image"` and an `imageId`. 23 files branch on `node.type`, and in every one an image behaves exactly as a shape does (select, resize, rotate, label, layers, groups, convert-to-nested, edges, copy and paste); only the three renderers differ, so a new type would have cost 23 changes and risked missing one silently. **Pixels live once, in a document-level `assets` table**, not as a `data:` URI in the style as draw.io does: a picture pasted five times is stored once, node JSON stays small and diff-friendly, and undo keeps sharing one asset object. Assets are never deleted with their node (undo needs them) and are pruned only when the document is encoded. **`formatVersion` 3**, with a no-op v2→v3 migration: the bump exists so an older app says "made with a newer version" rather than silently drawing blank shapes. **Size shaped the design more than anything**, and the code showed why: autosave writes the whole document to `localStorage` (~5 MB) and **fails silently** on overflow, so one photo could turn autosave off with no warning; share links cap at 16,000 characters, which any image exceeds; and autosave re-encodes the document 800 ms after every edit. Hence: always shrink to a 2,048 px long edge (user's choice over draw.io's per-image prompt), a 2 MB per-image cap, a visible "autosave is off" notice (fixing the silent failure for every large document, not only ones with images), and cached asset encoding. **One render path covers every export:** PNG, `.deepdraw.png`, PDF, print, PowerPoint, Word and copy-with-live-link all rasterise the SVG export, so drawing the asset as an SVG `<image>` with a `data:` URI (external URLs are blocked when an SVG is rasterised, and are ruled out anyway: no network) plus the canvas and the Nested Block miniature is the whole surface. **User decisions:** always shrink large images; a dropped PNG carrying a DeepDraw diagram still opens as that document (every other image is inserted, where today any dropped `.png` fails with an error alert); labels sit below the image, as in draw.io. **Out of scope:** URLs, cropping and editing, animation, images as fills or backgrounds, and mapping draw.io's own embedded images (a natural follow-up, to the Inbox once this lands). |
| D80 | 2026-09-28 | **Inserting your own image: built as scoped in D79, with five changes the building forced.** Eight commits, one per step of the scope's work breakdown. What ships: Arrange ▸ Insert image…, the canvas menu's Insert image… (placed where the menu opened), dropping files (at the drop point, several staggered), Ctrl+V of an image copied in another app, and Replace image… / Original size on a picture; an `assets` table with each picture stored once; the picture drawn identically on the canvas, in the SVG export and therefore in every export rasterised from it (PNG, `.deepdraw.png`, PDF, print, PowerPoint, Word, copy-with-live-link), and in Nested Block thumbnails; a Style tab section for pictures; and autosave that says when it stops fitting in browser storage. **(1) v3 only when needed.** The encoder always wrote `CURRENT_FORMAT_VERSION`, so bumping it would have stamped every newly saved file v3 and made diagrams without a single image unreadable by an older app; it now writes the lowest version that describes the document, and a share link's `v=` states the version its payload was actually written as — otherwise every new link would have been refused by an older app. The committed examples stayed byte-identical, which is the proof. **(2) Ctrl+V gained a fallback.** The paste event is the only way an image from another app arrives, and it never fires while the keydown is cancelled, so Ctrl+C/X/V stopped cancelling it. That made in-app shape paste depend on the paste event, which would have silently broken it anywhere a browser fires none; now Ctrl+V pastes the shapes itself after 150 ms without one. Copying shapes also marks the system clipboard, as draw.io does, so a stale screenshot left there cannot win a paste meant for shapes. The preview harness could not drive a native paste (its keys do not run the paste command); Playwright could, and the E2E proves the real path, since the fallback never inserts pictures. **(3) No encoding cache.** Measured before building it: a 4.4 MB document with three large pictures encodes in about 6 ms and writes to localStorage in about 11 ms, once per 800 ms autosave. **(4) Templates carry pictures**, re-issuing asset ids like every other id (D65) — found because the parity sweep's kitchen sink loads through `applyTemplate`, which would otherwise have dropped the table and left image nodes pointing at nothing. **(5) An image's label is always centred**, because the exporter cannot measure text and so could not reproduce left/right lines inside a centred block; label position is shared geometry (`canvas/imageGeometry.ts`) and the parity sweep compares it, along with picture presence and border, and reported both a dropped border and a 6 px label shift when each was introduced. **Found on the way, filed to the Inbox:** a render error in one small component (the status bar, mid-edit under hot reload) unmounted the whole shell, autosave included, with nothing on screen — there is no error boundary; the menus' Paste still reads only the in-app clipboard; and draw.io's embedded images can now be mapped on import. **Verified:** 994 unit tests, 151 E2E tests including the full parity sweep, five new E2E tests in real Chromium (menu with a real file chooser, drop, a real system-clipboard paste, save/reopen, undo across delete, and the picture's pixels counted in PNG and PowerPoint exports), and on screen in the running app. No new dependencies. |

Each region of the shell (menu bar, shape library, canvas, canvas panels, format panel, page tabs, status bar, dialogs) now has its own boundary showing a small notice with Try again, and the hooks — autosave among them — live in AppShell above them, so they keep running. Icons, pictures and nested blocks keep their box, now without the gap. The canvas stopped taking its ends from React Flow and calls the exporter's resolver with live node geometry, so the two are identical by construction; the parity sweep confirms it on all 35 templates. Measured on screen: 0.0–0.1px from the drawn outline on a rectangle, triangle, rounded rectangle, ellipse and diamond. **Harness lesson:** the preview pane stopped painting while in the background, so React Flow never measured handles and drew no edges at all — which looked exactly like a regression until the same code drew every edge under Playwright. C4, UML, ER and DFD stencils lost dividers a label could only collide with, and ER shapes now fill their box. **(4) `EdgeEnd.anchor`** — in the model since the start but never drawn — is honoured as a fixed connection point, which a sequence message needs to meet a lifeline at its own height. **(5) A layout audit** (`templates/layoutAudit.ts`) measures a diagram with the renderers' own geometry for a line through a shape it does not join, a line label on a shape, overlapping shapes, text wider than its shape, and two unrelated lines sharing a track. It found 129 flaws across the old set; every template now passes it, enforced by a catalogue test. **The templates.** Each was rebuilt to how its reference source draws it, and checked by the audit and by eye on rendered images: C4 in the published colours with a title and key per level; UML class boxes as grouped compartments, sequence messages pinned to their activation bars, ball-and-socket components; flowcharts with role colours and no dead ends; a two-sided mind map; DFDs with process numbers in their bands; crow's-foot tables with a key column; AWS, Azure, GCP and Kubernetes in their vendors' group conventions with lines joining icons at their sides, clear of the names hung beneath them; a perimeter network design; BPMN with a collapsed customer pool and fixed-width lane bands; a textbook fishbone, SWOT and canvas; a journey map with a persona and an emotion curve; a status-coloured roadmap; and trees and sitemaps in the indented work-breakdown style. **Why compose, not stencil:** a class, a table or a SWOT quadrant has parts a single label cannot fill, so those are built from grouped shapes, the way draw.io builds them, and each part is edited on its own. **Harness lesson:** the Bash tool collapses `\\n` inside a heredoc, so a Python patch that matched a template literal containing `\n` silently failed to match; scripted edits went into files instead. |
| D84 | 2026-10-03 | **Every format-panel and top-bar control vetted, and the shape library regrouped and filled out.** The user asked for every button in the Text tab, Arrange tab and top bar to be tested against what it claims, and every library icon checked for shape and grouping. **Controls.** An e2e sweep (`tests/e2e/m16-controls.spec.ts`) drove each control and found: number fields wrote 0 when cleared and recorded every keystroke as its own undo step; Ungroup was enabled on one plain shape; Help was a bare button so the quick tour could be seen only once, and starting the tour on a diagram without the sample ended it at once; `style.dash` was set by templates but never drawn; there was no Shadow or No-fill control; panel buttons used letters and emoji arrows. Fixes: shared `NumberField`/`ColorField`/`RangeField` (`format-panel/fields.tsx`) with a draft that ignores invalid input, clamps, and makes one edit one undo step (`useEditSession` pauses zundo between the first change and blur/pointer-up); `model/selectionGroup.ts` decides Group/Ungroup for menu, panel and context menu alike; a Help menu (shortcuts, quick tour that starts where it can and explains where it cannot, about); dash and shadow drawn on canvas and in SVG export (`canvas/shapeEffects.ts`), `shadow=1` imported from .drawio; drawn 16px icons for alignment, order, distribution and label position. **Library.** Checked against ISO 5807, BPMN 2.0, UML 2.5, C4 and common network-diagram practice. Flowchart now holds the whole ISO 5807 set: the 13 symbols that sat in Advanced moved in (old ids aliased in `RETIRED_STENCIL_IDS`, also used by the shared viewer through `resolveStencilId`), plus paper tape, direct access storage, sequential data and OR. Advanced keeps braces, brackets, frame, titled box, vertical swimlane and lines. Pictograms that did not belong in their section (a game controller among network devices, an undo arrow among diagram arrows, shape duplicates of Basic/Flowchart) are `hidden`: gone from picker and search, still drawn in saved diagrams. Added block arrows (the arrows diagrams actually use), BPMN start/intermediate/end event variants, user/service/send tasks, call activity, event gateway and group, UML combined fragment, decision and flow-final nodes, destruction, send signal and accept event, C4 database and queue, a load balancer, wireless access point, server rack and storage array, Basic star and cloud, and common status, security, dev and business icons from Material Symbols (Apache 2.0, already in NOTICE). Templates no longer use hidden shapes, enforced by the catalogue test. **Why hide rather than delete:** an unresolved shapeRef draws a blank box with no error, so nothing a saved diagram might use is ever removed. **No new dependencies.** |
| D85 | 2026-10-03 | **A Lines section: lines as shapes, with arrowheads that keep their proportions.** The user found no section of lines. 21 entries in three groups: the plain line (horizontal, vertical, diagonal, dashed, dotted, double, wavy, curve) and annotation arrows (one-way, two-way, open, dashed, curved, elbow); UML 2.5's four class relationships (inheritance, realization, composition, aggregation); and Information Engineering crow's-foot cardinality (one to one, one to many, many to many). Advanced's two plain lines moved here, old ids aliased; draw.io's `shape=line` imports as a Line. **Why shapes, not free-standing edges as draw.io's sidebar lines are:** a DeepDraw edge must join two nodes, and React Flow cannot draw an edge without both, so a loose edge would mean a model change and a `formatVersion` bump for something a shape does well — a rule, a divider, a pointer, a legend entry. Connecting two shapes stays what connectors are for. **How the heads stay true:** a stencil body is stretched to its box, which squashed any drawn arrowhead flat on a long thin line. A line stencil writes `<dd-line points=… start=… end=…>`, which `fitStencilBody` expands for the size it is drawn at (`library/stencils/lineBody.ts`): each head drawn in screen pixels, pointing along its end of the line, sized from the stroke width, in the stroke colour, never dashed, with the line stopping inside it as a connector's does. The heads are the connector's own (`canvas/arrowheads.ts`), so a hollow triangle on a line shape matches the one in Style › Arrows; picker tiles draw them 2.2× larger so the variants can be told apart at 40px. **`StencilDef.defaultStyle`:** style copied onto a placed shape, so a dashed line arrives with `dash: "dashed"` and the Style panel's Line pattern says so, and the user can change it. **More Shapes setting:** the enabled-sections preference was a bare list of ids, which cannot tell a section the user switched off from one added since — anyone who had ever hidden a section would never have seen Lines. It now stores the sections it knew of too; a bare list is read as predating Lines. **No new dependencies.** |
| D86 | 2026-10-03 | **Lines are lines: free ends, draw.io's line handling, and a line toolbar (supersedes D85's lines-as-shapes).** The user compared D85's Lines section with draw.io in a screen recording: there a sidebar line is a real line, not a box — its ends drag onto shapes to connect and off them again, its midpoint drags to bend it, and a small bar over it changes its pattern, ends and route. **Model, formatVersion 4.** `EdgeEnd` is attached (`nodeId`) or free (`point`), exactly one, enforced by validate.ts (`BAD_EDGE_END`). A document is written as v4 only when it has a free end, so others still open in older apps; no migration is needed, since every older edge stays valid. Every place that assumed an edge joins two shapes now handles a free end: deletion, convert-to-nested (a free outer end makes a boundary edge with a port), ghost links, template application, the layout audit, find, miniatures, and the SVG/PNG/PDF exports, which resolve free ends in the same shared routing code as the canvas (`freeEndPosition`: a free end leaves toward its neighbour). **Canvas.** React Flow cannot draw an edge without a node at each end, so each free end gets an invisible one-pixel anchor node (`canvas/PointNode.tsx`), built from the edges every render and never part of the document; the edge draws from the model's own point. DiagramEdge replaces React Flow's reconnect handles with draw.io's: end dots (solid when free, hollow when attached) that drop onto a shape anywhere on its body (nearest side, `canvas/dropTarget.ts`, with the shape outlined while hovering) or stay free where let go; faint midpoint dots that bend the line; a live coordinate readout; and a free line moves as a whole when dragged by its body. A connection started from a shape and let go short of a handle now attaches to the shape it was dropped on, or stays as a line from the shape on open canvas — both were silently discarded before. The edge-label layer sits above the shapes, so an end dot resting on a shape's connection dot can be grabbed. **Line toolbar** (`canvas/EdgeToolbar.tsx`), over a line selected on its own, at a fixed screen size: Line style (pattern, width, colour), Line ends (each end's arrowhead picked from drawn previews of the same arrowhead set as everywhere else), Connection (straight/elbow/curved route; line, double line, block arrow or wavy line; clear bends). Popups open below the bar so they never run off the top of the canvas, and stay open across picks. **Line shapes** (`style.edgeShape`, `canvas/edgeShapes.ts`, shared by canvas and export): `double` and `arrow` (a hollow block arrow along the route) are a wide stroke with the page colour laid down its middle, the arrow's heads outlined and its body stopped at their base; `wavy` is a wave along the line's stretches, straight for the first and last half-wave so an arrowhead points along the line. draw.io's `link`/`flexArrow` import as these, and draw.io's dangling edges (`sourcePoint`/`targetPoint`) now import as free lines instead of being skipped. **Sidebar.** Every Lines entry places a line (`StencilDef.line`, scaled to the drop size) — the wavy line included, after the user pointed out it was still a box — plus a new Block Arrow. Diagrams saved during D85 keep drawing those lines as boxes. **Also:** Ctrl+C/V/D copy lines (a selected line, and every connection between copied shapes, re-pointed at the copies — connections used to be lost on copy); arrow keys nudge selected lines; a rubber band selects a line whose free ends it encloses; a page holding only lines is not treated as empty by the start panel, the template browser or Help › Quick Tour. **Selection race:** React Flow reports an empty selection while a newly placed line's anchors arrive ahead of the line, and the delayed clear (the box-select flicker fix) then deselected it; the clear is now dropped if the selection changed meanwhile or React Flow still shows something selected. **No new dependencies.** |

D31 collapsed every section on a first visit so a dozen open shape grids wouldn't overwhelm a newcomer, but that left the sidebar as a column of closed headers with no shape in sight to start from. draw.io opens its General section, and so does this now (`library/collapsedSections.ts`; the same default applies if storage can't be read). Only the first-visit default changed: once someone opens or closes any section, their choice is kept exactly as before, General included. E2E: `m18-sidebar-default.spec.ts`. **No new dependencies.** |
| D88 | 2026-10-03 | **A line's width has its own named row, and lines default to 1pt.** Reported by the user from a screenshot: in the Style tab the width sat as a bare number beside the colour swatch with no name of its own, and lines were drawn 1.75 wide by default, which read heavy beside shapes' 1px outlines. Width now has its own row, named and followed by "pt", for lines, shape outlines and picture borders alike. The default comes from one constant (`DEFAULT_EDGE_WIDTH = 1` in canvas/edgeRouting.ts), read by the canvas, the Style tab, the line toolbar and every export, matching draw.io's 1pt. | D89 | 2026-10-04 | **Three AI-infrastructure cloud templates, one per vendor, each a different kind of AI system.** The user asked for one more template per cloud, a variation on the existing ones themed on AI infrastructure, and built to industry standard. Each follows that vendor's own published reference rather than a generic picture, and the three differ on purpose, so a reader sees the three shapes an AI system commonly takes. **AWS generative AI assistant** (`aws-bedrock-rag`): retrieval-augmented generation as AWS Prescriptive Guidance draws it with Knowledge Bases for Amazon Bedrock. CloudFront and WAF lead to API Gateway, which checks the Cognito token, then a Lambda orchestrator in a private subnet reaches Bedrock only through a PrivateLink endpoint. The knowledge base runs vector search over OpenSearch Serverless before the foundation model answers, and Guardrails screen prompts and answers. Ingestion runs along the bottom: S3 documents are chunked and embedded into the index. DynamoDB holds the chat history, and KMS and CloudWatch form the operations box. **Azure baseline AI chat** (`azure-foundry-chat`): Microsoft's Baseline Microsoft Foundry chat reference architecture. A VNet's subnets are drawn as columns: Application Gateway with WAF, App Service integration, private endpoints, and Azure Firewall. App Service calls the Foundry Agent Service through a private endpoint with its managed identity; the agent fans out to Azure OpenAI, AI Search, Cosmos DB and Storage, and its internet tool calls leave only through the firewall. Key Vault, managed identities, Application Insights and Log Analytics form the operations and identity plane. **GCP ML platform** (`gcp-ml-platform`): Google's MLOps on Vertex AI with the GKE inference reference. BigQuery features and Cloud Storage datasets feed Vertex AI Pipelines, training runs on AI Hypercomputer, and models are registered, then deployed to GKE GPU node pools behind an Apigee API. Vertex AI Model Monitoring reads prediction logs and triggers retraining on drift, closing the loop. Every vendor icon comes from the vendor's own pack. Users, client apps and external tools are vendor-neutral, since no vendor icon represents them, and the GCP pack's category icon stands in for Cloud Monitoring, as in gcpWebApp. All three pass the layout audit (D83) and were checked by eye on rendered images. architecture.test.ts adds the claims each reference turns on: AWS keeps the orchestrator inside the VPC and the managed services outside it, and reaches Bedrock only through the endpoint. Azure keeps PaaS outside the VNet and egress through the firewall. GCP closes the drift-to-retrain loop. **No new dependencies.** |

| D91 | 2026-10-04 | **Redesign R2: the top bar becomes an app menu, a command palette, an Export split button and a Share button**, replacing draw.io's File · Edit · View · Arrange · Extras · Help menu bar (spec `docs/DeepDraw_Redesign_Spec_20261004_v1.0.md`; the five open choices below were approved in review). **Layout.** Three columns so the breadcrumb stays centred whatever is either side: on the left the app menu (the mark plus `BRAND.name` ▾) and a "Search or run a command" button (Ctrl K); the breadcrumb in the middle; on the right **Export** and **Share**. **App menu:** New diagram, New from template…, Open… (Ctrl+O), Save a copy (Ctrl+S), **Recent**, Preferences…, Keyboard shortcuts, Take the tour, About. **Command palette** (`app/commands.ts` is the one registry it reads, built on demand from the store, so a command is disabled or ticked exactly when its menu item was): every command the six menus held, under the menus' own labels, filtered as you type (every word must appear in the label, group or keywords), Up/Down/Enter/Esc, focus returned on close. With a query it also lists "Go to: <name>" for every labelled shape and Nested Block on **every level and page** (`app/goTo.ts`), which navigates there, selects it and fits the view. **Export split button:** the main part repeats the last format used in this browser (`${storagePrefix}:lastExport`, a per-viewer convenience), **"Export PNG" until then**; its arrow lists Copy image with live link, every format with its extension (PowerPoint and Word first, then Image, Editable image, Vector, PDF, the native file), and Print…. **Share** is a primary button opening the existing share dialog. **The five decisions.** (1) **Recent** keeps the last **8** documents themselves in IndexedDB (`file/recentDocs.ts`; a browser cannot reopen a file path without asking again, and localStorage's ~5 MB would hold two pictures), stored as `encodeDoc` output so reopening goes through `decodeDoc`'s validation like opening a file. `loadDoc` remembers the *outgoing* document whenever it has content (not in the read-only viewer, not the onboarding sample), so New, Open, a template or a Recent entry can never lose work — New's confirm now says so. Entries are keyed by `meta.created`, so the same diagram replaces its own entry. Nothing leaves the browser; the local-only claim stands. (2) **Preferences** is a small dialog holding Dark mode (the only per-user display setting today). (3) **Layers** and **Outline** join Grid and Snap on the canvas chip as `aria-pressed` toggles, as well as being in the palette. (4) The Export main button behaves as above. (5) **Ctrl+O and Ctrl+S** are added (the browser's own open/save would otherwise fire); **Ctrl+N is not**, because browsers keep it for a new window. **Ctrl+K** opens the palette — nothing in the app used it. **Tour and tip retargeted:** tour step 3 (D63) spotlights the Export button and names "Export ▾ › Copy image with live link"; its "Show me" opens the Export menu (`ui.exportMenuRequest`, renamed from `fileMenuRequest`) and the card now sits to the menu's *left*, since the menu opens leftward from the top-right corner. The first-PNG tip (D64) points at Export ▾. The quick tour moved into the app menu. **Menu primitive** rewritten to the WAI-ARIA menu pattern (role menu/menuitem/menuitemcheckbox, focus on open, arrows/Home/End, Esc returns focus to the trigger), so both menus are fully keyboard-operable. **Tests:** the Edit, View, Arrange and Extras menus' unit tests moved into `CommandPalette.test.tsx` with the same assertions (a greyed menu item is a palette option with `aria-disabled`; a ✓ is still a ✓); File and Help tests moved to the app menu; every E2E that opened a menu now opens the app menu, the Export menu or the palette (`runCommand` helper); new `m20-top-bar.spec.ts` covers Ctrl+K, Go to into a Nested Block, the Export button remembering its format across a reload, Recent restoring a replaced diagram, Preferences and Ctrl+S. The R2 inventory (every menu command → its new location) is in the PR description. **No new dependencies** (IndexedDB is a platform API). |
| D92 | 2026-10-05 | **Redesign R3: ＋ handles replace the four hover arrows.** draw.io shows four blue arrows on hover; clicking one clones the shape on that side and connects the two, and dragging one connects to another shape (Alt+Shift+Arrow clones and connects from the keyboard). DeepDraw keeps both gestures under its own look: hovering a shape or Nested Block shows a violet **＋ just outside the middle of each side** (the four corner perimeter points stay). **Drag ＋** connects exactly as before. Each ＋ is drawn *inside* its side handle, reaching outward, so the handle — its id (`top`/`right`/`bottom`/`left`) and its place on the border — is unchanged: edges already attached to a side stay put, connection snapping and drop targets are untouched, and edge ends still come from shape geometry (`resolveHandleAnchor`, D81), never the handle's DOM position. **Click ＋** adds a connected copy (`model/connectedCopy.ts`, store `addConnectedCopy`): same shape and size, the stencil's **default style** (not the original's formatting, per the spec), a picture keeps its picture, and a Nested Block's copy is a **new, empty Nested Block** with its own child diagram (a child diagram has one parent), connected through ports like any edge onto a block. The copy sits 64px (four grid cells) beyond that side, in line with the original; **snap moves it only along the way it goes**, so a copy of an off-grid shape stays aligned rather than jumping 8px sideways; if something already sits there it steps further out, so repeated clicks make a chain. The edge uses the default edge style from that side to the copy's facing side. The copy is selected with its **label open for typing**, and the copy, its edge and any ports are **one undo step**. The click replaces React Flow's click-to-connect on those four handles (never documented or tested here). **Found while building:** a copy placed off screen was never drawn (the canvas renders only what is visible), so its label could not open; the canvas now pans just enough to bring it into view (`revealRect`, as draw.io scrolls a clone into view). And a label editor opened on a brand-new node lost its focus, because React Flow keeps a node hidden until measured; the editor now retries focus for a few frames. **Size on screen:** the ＋ keeps one size whatever the zoom, as draw.io's arrows do, via a `--dd-inverse-zoom` CSS variable written straight onto the canvas element from React Flow's store (`ZoomVariable.tsx`), so zooming re-renders no node. **Keyboard and accessibility:** Alt+Shift+Arrow (draw.io's clone-and-connect) and four palette commands, "Add connected shape to the right / below / to the left / above", do the same for the one selected shape; each ＋ has an accessible name and a tooltip. The ＋ is hidden outright (not just transparent) when not hovered, and never shown in a shared link's read-only viewer. **Tests:** `connectedCopy.test.ts` (placement, snap, stepping out, default style, pictures, Nested Blocks, groups, one undo step, viewer); palette and Alt+Shift+Arrow cases; new `m21-plus-handles.spec.ts` (four ＋ outside each side, click → copy named by typing then undone in one step, drag from ＋ connects, constant size at 50% and 200%, Nested Block copy, the shortcut, the viewer). Existing connection specs pass unchanged. **No new dependencies.** |
| D93 | 2026-10-05 | **Redesign R4: the shape library organised by purpose.** draw.io lists its libraries as a flat run of sections (General, Misc, Advanced, …) with "More Shapes…" to choose which show. DeepDraw's sidebar now reads: search, **Recently used**, Favorites, then nine **purpose groups**, then **Browse all libraries**. The mapping, approved in review before any code: **Basics** = General, Basic, Advanced · **Lines & Arrows** = Lines, Arrows · **Flow** = Flowchart, BPMN · **Data** = Entity Relation, Data Flow (DFD) · **Software** = UML, C4 · **Cloud** = Azure, AWS, GCP · **Network** = Network · **People & Business** = Business · **Icons** = Misc; Favorites stays its own heading. All 18 sections are placed exactly once (`libraryGroups.test.ts` asserts it). **Groups are presentation only** (`library/libraryGroups.ts`): every section and stencil id is unchanged, and everything already stored keys off section ids — Favorites, which sections are open, which are enabled, template shapeRefs, the stencil-coverage test — so nothing needed migrating; nothing was ever keyed by label. A group of several sections shows each as an indented sub-heading that opens and closes on its own, on the existing per-section state (so opening Cloud does not render 1,064 icons at once); a group of one section *is* that section and uses its state. Each group heading shows how many shapes it holds, once its sections have loaded. **Open/closed groups** are a new per-browser setting (`${storagePrefix}:collapsedGroups`). Its first value is derived — a group starts closed only if every section in it was closed — and saved at once, so a returning user's sidebar shows what it showed before, and from then on a group changes only when its own heading is clicked (closing General must not make Basics vanish on the next load). **D87 carries over:** on a first visit only Basics is open, and only General inside it — the same 15 shapes a newcomer saw before. **Recently used** (`library/recentShapes.ts`, `${storagePrefix}:recentShapes`): the last 8 different stencils placed from the library by click or drag, lines included, newest first; hidden until there is one; its tiles place and drag exactly like the library's. Favorites are not recorded (they are already one click away and carry their own style). **Browse all libraries** replaces More Shapes… and does the same job on the same stored setting, now laid out under the group headings, as a labelled modal dialog that Esc closes; a group whose sections are all switched off disappears from the sidebar. `sectionLabel` now spells BPMN and Data Flow (DFD) properly where it used to fall back to "Bpmn" and "Dfd". **Raised in review:** "Basic" under "Basics" read oddly, and the sub-headings had inherited the old all-caps section style while the group titles were sentence case. So inside Basics the sections are shown as **Essentials** (General), **Geometric** (Basic) and **Frames & Brackets** (Advanced) — display labels on the group (`sectionLabels`, used by the sidebar and Browse all libraries alike), ids unchanged — and every heading is now sentence case: Favorites styled as a top-level heading like the groups, sub-headings quieter and indented. **Tests:** new `libraryGroups.test.ts` (mapping, every section once, derived and saved state) and `recentShapes.test.ts`; ShapeLibrary tests for groups, single-section groups, counts, Recently used, first visit and the renamed dialog; `m18-sidebar-default.spec.ts` updated to the groups, plus a group surviving a reload and Recently used across a reload. **No new dependencies.** |
| D94 | 2026-10-05 | **Redesign R5: silent restore.** The app used to open on "Unsaved diagram found — restore?"; nearly everyone answered yes, and the one who answered no lost their work. Now autosaved work simply comes back (`app/RestoreOnLoad.tsx`, replacing `RestorePrompt.tsx`), with a small note — **"Restored your last diagram"** and a **Start new** button — that fades after six seconds, but not while the pointer or keyboard focus is on it (it is a `role="status"` region). An empty autosave is restored without a note. **Start new never destroys the restored work silently — approach: keep it under Recent.** Start new first stores the restored diagram in Recent (the IndexedDB store R2 added, D91) and *awaits* that, then starts a blank diagram and says "Your previous one is under Recent in the app menu". Chosen over "confirm if it was never exported" because it costs the user nothing — no dialog in the common case, and the work is one click away in the app menu — while a confirm on every Start new would bring back the very question R5 removes. Only when this browser cannot keep it (no IndexedDB, or the write fails) does Start new ask first. `rememberDocument` now reports whether it stored the document so that difference can be told. **Unreadable autosave: surfaced, never dropped.** The spec asked to keep "today's behaviour of surfacing the problem", but there was none: Restore on an autosave that failed validation did nothing, and the next autosave then overwrote it — a silent loss. Now such an autosave is set aside under its own key (`${storagePrefix}:autosaveUnreadable`) the moment it is found, so later autosaves cannot overwrite it, and a banner (`role="alert"`) says it couldn't be restored, with decodeDoc's reason, a **Download it** button (the raw file, for recovery by hand) and **Dismiss**. It is shown again on every visit until dismissed. **Unchanged:** the first-visit sample and tour still load only when there is no autosave; a share link's viewer, and a copy taken from it, never restore or touch autosave. **Tests:** new `RestoreOnLoad.test.tsx` (restore with note, nothing autosaved, empty autosave, fade and hover hold, Start new keeps it under Recent first, Start new asks when it cannot, unreadable set aside, shown again until dismissed); App tests updated to the new test ids; m4's restore E2E now expects the work back with no prompt; new `m22-silent-restore.spec.ts` (note and fade, Start new then reopen from Recent, unreadable autosave downloaded and kept across reloads until dismissed). **No new dependencies.** |
| D95 | 2026-10-05 | **Redesign R6: DeepDraw's own default look, for new shapes and edges only.** draw.io draws a new shape white with a black outline and ends a connection in a filled triangle; DeepDraw's look, matching the mockup and approved in review: outline shapes keep their **white fill and black (ink) outline** and are placed with their **shadow on** (`newShapeStyle` writes `shadow: true` into a new outline shape's style — explicit, so nothing already drawn can gain one, and the Shadow control shows it on; pictograms, vendor icons, pictures and lines get none); edges default to **slate `#7A89A1`** (a `--dd-color-edge` token, `#8F9CB2` in dark mode); the **Rounded Rectangle** is rounder (≈10px at its default size, from ≈7px; a `lookBody` on the stencil, the plain Rectangle stays square); and new connections end in an **open chevron** (`endArrow: "open"`, an existing choice). **Unchanged:** every width and size, font size, the D78 label-contrast rules (labels stay ink on the tint), pictograms, vendor icons, and stencils with a colour of their own (BPMN pools, C4 boundaries). **Rule 2 — every existing document renders identically — by a per-element marker, `style.look = "classic"`** (`model/look.ts`). Unset fills, outlines and edge colours were runtime fallbacks, so changing them alone would have restyled every saved diagram. The spec offered writing explicit styles into old documents; that cannot work for edges, whose default colour is a *theme variable* (lighter in dark mode) that no literal in the file could keep. So the format goes to **v5**, and the v4→v5 migration marks every shape and edge in an older document classic; every resolver — canvas, SVG/PNG/PDF export (and through it PowerPoint and Word), miniatures, Favorites, the format panel and label contrast — takes the marker into account through the same functions (`getStencilDefaultStyle(stencil, classic)`, `stencilBody`, `edgeColor.ts`), so canvas and export cannot drift. Per element, not per document, so a shape copied out of an old diagram keeps its look in a new one. A document with anything unmarked is written as v5: an older app would draw it classic, and — the trap — a v4 file read back here would be migrated to classic, silently restyling new work. **Proof:** a document drawn, and its SVG export rendered, by the code on main just before R6 is committed (`src/file/migrations/fixtures/pre-r6.*`), and `look.test.ts` asserts today's code exports it **byte for byte** the same (which caught one real difference: the classic edge colour's letter case). **Rule 3 — templates unchanged:** every template is marked classic as it loads (`loadTemplate`), so the catalogue, previews, applied diagrams and the render-parity sweep all draw them as before; the three committed `examples/` are marked the same way and regenerated (the only change in those files is the added marker). The **first-visit sample** is not a template and shows the new look, as approved. **Raised in review:** the mockup's tint `#F3F6FA` sat almost on the canvas grey `#F6F7F9`, so new shapes barely separated from it; a stronger tint was tried and then dropped at the user's call — a tint added nothing over plain white — so the fill stays **white**; a slate outline was then dropped too — outlines stay **black** — and new shapes get a **shadow** instead, both at the user's call. Edges keep the slate, a step darker than the mockup's, `#7A89A1` (values above are the final ones). And Basics › Essentials, home of the Nested Block, is now opened once in every browser (`revealEssentialsOnce`, `${storagePrefix}:essentialsShown`) — a sidebar saved before the groups could have it closed — after which the user's own choice is respected. **Screenshots** of a new diagram before/after in light and dark are in `docs/redesign/r6/`. **Tests:** `look.test.ts` (the byte-for-byte pre-R6 export, the migration marking exactly shapes and edges, written back at its old version, the new defaults, stencils with their own colour untouched, the rounder corner on that shape only, edge colours on canvas and in exports, open chevron and v5 for new work, every template classic, a template staying classic once applied, the stamp idempotent); version and default expectations in five existing test files updated to say which look they mean. **No new dependencies.** |
| D96 | 2026-10-05 | **New edges are black, like shape outlines.** After R6 (D95) dropped the slate outline for black, the user asked for edges to match. New edges default to the ink `#1E2733` (`EDGE_COLOR`, exports), and on screen to a `--dd-color-edge` token that is the ink in light mode and the light ink `#E6E9EE` in dark mode — a literally black edge would vanish on the dark canvas, the same reason pictograms follow the theme. Classic-marked edges (every document from before v5, templates, examples) keep the muted grey, so nothing existing changes; no format change, since the default lives in code and the classic marker already separates the two. **No new dependencies.** |
| D97 | 2026-10-05 | **Draggable edge labels, as in draw.io.** draw.io lets an edge label be dragged anywhere and stores it relative to the edge — a position along it plus an offset — so it follows the shapes. Ours sat fixed at the middle. **Model:** optional `DEdge.labelPosition: { t, dx, dy }` — `t` of the way along the line by length (0 at the source), then `dx`,`dy` off it; validated (`t` in 0..1, finite offsets); omitted means the middle, exactly as before. Additive, so **no `formatVersion` bump** (an older app shows the label in the middle, as `pageSize` and `background` before it). **Geometry:** `buildEdgePath` places the label (`pathPointAt` on the same sampled polyline the midpoint always used, so an unmoved label is byte-identical) and now also returns the line's own `midPos`, which the bend dot and the line toolbar use instead of the label's spot. Canvas, every export and the template layout audit share it, so they cannot disagree. **Gesture:** pressing a label and moving 3px drags it; where it lands is the nearest point on the line (`nearestOnPath`) plus the offset, kept with the grip so the label does not jump to the pointer; within 6px of the line it sits on the line; values are rounded (t to 4 places, offsets to 0.1) so files stay readable. One undo step on release. A press without movement selects the line, and double-click still edits. **Found while building:** React Flow's `screenToFlowPosition` snaps to the grid when snap is on, so the label lagged the pointer by up to half a grid cell (60px of drag moved it 48); the label drag asks for unsnapped positions, as draw.io does not grid-snap labels. **Reset:** "Reset label position" in a line's right-click menu and in the palette (Arrange) puts selected labels back in the middle. Read-only viewer: no dragging. **Tests:** `edgeLabelPosition.test.ts` (path geometry, snapping to the line, rounding, `buildEdgePath`'s label vs middle, store undo/reset/viewer, file round-trip, validation, the export drawing the label where it was moved); `m23-edge-labels.spec.ts` (drag, follows a moved shape, undo, click selects, double-click edits, reset from the menu). **No new dependencies.** |
| D98 | 2026-10-05 | **The label editor's own formatting strip is gone.** Raised in review with a screenshot: while typing a label, a white strip (bold, italic, size, colour, align) sat under the black context bar, and "white label is not doing anything that black label is not doing already" — R1 (D90) had moved exactly those controls into the bar's Text popover, and the strip predated it. Removed. Nothing is lost: the popover has every control, and clicking into the bar first commits the text being typed (the editor commits on blur), so the two can never overwrite each other's changes. Ctrl+B and Ctrl+I still work while typing. **Fixed with it:** the editor used to commit a size (12) and an alignment into every label it touched, so editing an edge label — drawn at 11 by default — quietly made it 12; it now writes back the label as it was with only the new text (and bold/italic). **Tests:** the editor shows no formatting controls, types in the colour the label shows, writes back only the text and what was already set, and Ctrl+B/Ctrl+I still apply. **No new dependencies.** |
| D99 | 2026-10-05 | **Style presets, as a trial.** draw.io's format panel opens with a grid of ready-made styles that set a shape's fill, outline and font colour in one click. The user asked to build it and "see if its worth keeping". **What:** a row of eight swatches at the top of the context bar's Fill popover (`format-panel/stylePresets.ts`): **Default**, then **Blue, Green, Amber, Red, Violet, Grey** — each a light fill with a deeper outline of the same hue — and **Dark**. Our own palette, not draw.io's. A preset sets **fill and outline only**: the label already takes a contrasting colour from the fill (D78), so text stays readable on every preset, white on Dark, with nothing to set or undo. **Default** *removes* both, so the shape returns to the standard look (white, black outline) rather than being pinned to today's values. One store action, `applyStylePreset`, so a click on several shapes is **one undo step**; it leaves every other key alone (shadow, dash, opacity). Applied to the outline shapes in the selection only: a pictogram or vendor icon keeps its own colours, which a tint would wash out. The swatch matching the first shape is marked (`aria-pressed`), each has an accessible name ("Style: Blue"), and the row is keyboard-reachable like the rest of the popover. No format change: presets write ordinary `fill`/`stroke` values. **Tests:** `stylePresets.test.tsx` (the set, matching, one undo step, Default clearing only those two keys, viewer, the row applying and marking a preset, a pictogram left alone in a mixed selection) and an m19 E2E case. **No new dependencies.** |
| D100 | 2026-10-06 | **Renamed from DeepDraw to Nedito, and moved from myconifer.app to nedito.app.** **Why:** another live product, deepdraw.app, writes `.deepdraw.json`, `.deepdraw.html` and `.deepdraw.png` files — the same name and, for the editable image, the very same extension as ours. Nedito is short for Nested Diagram Tool. **Identity** (`src/app/brand.ts`, the one place, D10): `BRAND` = name Nedito, `fileExt` `.nedito` (editable image `.nedito.png`), `formatId` `nedito`, `storagePrefix` `nedito`, and a new `host` `nedito.app` with `APP_ORIGIN`; every earlier identity sits in `LEGACY_BRANDS` (DeepDraw: `.deepdraw`, `deepdraw`, hosts myconifer.app and www.myconifer.app) and is **read forever, never written**. CSS classes, custom properties and test ids moved from `dd-` to `nd-`. Code identifiers that carried the name became brand-neutral rather than renamed to the new one (`DeepDrawDoc` → `DiagramDocument`, `deepdrawFormat.ts` → `documentFormat.ts`, `deepdrawPngFormat.ts` → `editablePngFormat.ts`), so the next rename really is one file. **No `formatVersion` bump.** The format rules bump on breaking *schema* changes; the id is not schema. Old files decode by `KNOWN_FORMAT_IDS` (current + legacy); an app that predates the rename refuses a `"nedito"` file on its id before it ever looks at the version, so a bump could not give it a better message. **Old things that keep working, each tested:** `.deepdraw` and `.deepdraw.png` files and the `deepdraw` id open (picker and drop accept both extensions); share-link payloads written with the old id decode; Word exports' embedded document part, written now as `<nedito xmlns="https://nedito.app/ns/document">`, is still read in its old `<deepdraw xmlns="https://deepdraw.local/document">` form; the last-export choice stored under its old ids (`deepdraw-png`, `deepdraw`) maps to the new ones. The PNG chunk keyword `dd-doc` is deliberately unchanged: it is a container key chosen in D36 precisely to survive a rename, and every existing editable image carries it. Saving writes only `.nedito`/`.nedito.png` and `nedito`. **Storage** (`app/storageMigration.ts`, imported first in `main.tsx` via `app/startup.ts`, before any module reads a setting): every `deepdraw:` key — autosave, theme, Favorites, recently used shapes, sidebar and section state, tour state, the sample-seen flag, last export, tip flags — is copied to `nedito:` unless the new key exists; a record under `nedito:migratedFrom` lists them; the originals are removed only on a later load, once every copy is confirmed intact. The Recent list's IndexedDB database `deepdraw-recent` is copied into `nedito-recent` and deleted only when every entry is confirmed there (`migrateLegacyRecent`). **Hosts** (`app/appOrigin.ts`): share links and the live link in a copied image are built on `APP_ORIGIN` from any production host, and on the page itself elsewhere (dev, tests, previews). The Office exports link only inside themselves (bookmarks, slides), so carry no host. Page title, description, canonical and `og:url`, and the web manifest (`start_url` and `scope` relative "/") are generated from `BRAND` by a Vite plugin (`vite.brand.ts`) — no host or name literal in `index.html`. **Batch A** (asked to "carry forward"): no such www/apex work existed in this repo, so it was built here, generalised to every alias host (`app/originGuard.ts`): www.nedito.app and myconifer.app send the visitor to the same path, query and `#` fragment on nedito.app — the whole shared diagram travels in that fragment, which never reaches a server — *unless* that origin holds the visitor's own work (an autosave with anything drawn, or an unreadable one, under either prefix), which nedito.app cannot read: then a notice says the app has moved and offers **Download my diagram** first; going on without it asks for confirmation. Seen once per autosave, so later visits go straight through. A server-side redirect would have preserved the fragment too, but would have stranded that work, which is why the redirect lives in the app. **Left as written:** the Cloudflare worker name `deepdraw` in `wrangler.jsonc` (renaming deploys a second worker and orphans the one serving myconifer.app; domains are attached in the dashboard); the GitHub repository name; CLAUDE_HISTORY.md entries; PROGRESS.md's past entries; the dated spec documents (`docs/DeepDraw_*`, `portable-diagrams-spec.md`, `onboarding-spec.md`), which are records of what was asked; the pre-R6 fixture `pre-r6.deepdraw`, a document as written before the rename and the proof it still renders; the dev-only `window.__ddStore` hook. The R6 golden SVG's internal marker ids moved to `nd-` with the exporter (ids, not drawing). **Tests:** `rename.test.ts` (old id, old extensions, old editable PNG, old share payload; only new written; an old-prefix profile carried over, kept until confirmed, then removed; new key never overwritten; old last-export id; Recent copied; a myconifer.app share URL redirected with path and fragment intact and still decoding; www.nedito.app; redirect vs notice; acknowledged once per autosave; link base per host), `MovedNotice.test.tsx`, docx legacy part, and `m24-rename.spec.ts` (an old-prefix profile in a real browser, an old file opened and saved as `.nedito`, `.nedito.png`, title/canonical/og:url/manifest). **No new dependencies.** |
| D101 | 2026-10-06 | **The Nedito icon, mark and favicon (PR 2 of the Nedito sequence).** The user supplied three SVGs. **Where they live:** the app icon (violet tile) and the 16px favicon are static files in a new `public/icons/` (Vite copies `public/` as is); the mark, which must take its surface's text colour, is `src/app/brand-mark.svg`, imported `?raw` and inlined by `BrandMark` — an `<img>` cannot inherit `currentColor`. Renamed to brand-neutral file names (`app-icon.svg`, `favicon-16.svg`, `brand-mark.svg`) so a future rename leaves asset paths alone (D10, as D100). The files keep the content-provenance (C2PA) metadata they arrived with; the inlined mark drops it from the page only, and only the root `<svg>` loses its fixed size and label (an early version stripped the inner shapes' sizes too and drew nothing — caught visually). **PNGs generated, not drawn:** `scripts/generate-icons.mjs` (`npm run icons`) renders each from its SVG at exact size, transparent, in the Chromium Playwright already installs — no new dependency: `icon-192.png` and `icon-512.png` for the manifest, `apple-touch-icon-180.png` for iOS (which ignores the manifest), and `favicon-32.png` from the thick-stroked 16px drawing as the raster fallback. The icon is `public/icons/app-icon.svg` itself, read at build and only marked up to animate (D101: never redrawn); colours and the font stack are read out of `tokens.css`. **Which screen:** the head script, before the first paint — a share link gets none (`display:none` before it can paint, so not even a frame in the viewer); nothing stored is a first visit; the seen flag `nedito:splashSeen`, or an autosave under either name (so not even the pre-migration first load after the rename holds a returning user), makes it a later visit; unreadable storage counts as a later visit, since the flag could never be stored. **When it leaves** (`app/splash.ts`): a first visit holds until 1.8s from navigation start so the name registers, then goes once the canvas is ready (3s at the latest, so a failed load is never hidden); any key or click ends the hold. A later visit goes the moment the canvas is ready (DiagramCanvas's `onInit`, after the frame it paints in), 1s at the latest, and draws nothing for its first 150ms — a fast load shows plain canvas colour and then the canvas, never a flash of the screen. Reduced motion keeps the screen and drops the motion and the fades. **No layout shift:** every size is fixed — icon, column width (a text-width column moved the icon by 0.016px when the font arrived; caught by the test), both line heights — and the text appears only once IBM Plex has loaded, so it never paints in a fallback face first. Decorative (`aria-hidden`); the app announces itself once loaded. Dark mode from the stored theme. **Tests:** `splash.test.ts` (the decision, the self-contained head script, the page parts, every timing path with a fake clock) and `m26-startup.spec.ts` (never up in the viewer for a frame; first visit up from the first frame, held ~1.8s, then a later visit's short screen; no box moves by a pixel; a restored diagram's node clickable the instant it shows and the screen gone within a frame of the canvas; on a first visit a click reaches the canvas under the screen; reduced motion). Also fixed two spec links the rename broke (CLAUDE.md, `templates/types.ts`): the dated `docs/DeepDraw_*` spec files keep their names. **No new dependencies.** |

| D116 | 2026-10-07 | **A1: diagram motion's model, and Flow and Pulse.** Plan approved by the user. draw.io's only motion is a per-edge animated-dash style key; Nedito's is a typed, optional `motion` object (`model/motion.ts`): `DEdge.motion` (flow / packets / throughput, speed, volume), `NodeBase.motion` (pulse with an optional ripple, health), `NestedNode.peek`, `Diagram.motion` (drawIn, one presentation mode with its order — exclusive by the shape of the data). **The whole approved model is defined and validated now**, though A1 draws only Flow and Pulse, so format v6 never changes shape as later PRs add renderers: a file using Packets from A3 still validates in an A1-era app. **Format v6 only when used** (`versionFor`, like v3–v5): a document without motion is written byte for byte as before; the v5→v6 migration restamps; FORMAT.md documents it. Edits are pure (`setEdgesMotion`, `setNodesMotion`), each one undo entry, and clearing motion removes the key rather than leaving it empty. **Who decides:** reduced motion stops everything, and a Motion switch (Preferences in the editor, a Motion button in the share-link viewer's strip, shown only when the diagram has motion) is remembered per browser; every animated element carries `nd-anim`, so one stylesheet (`styles/motion.css`) stops it all, and low detail (P1) stops it too. `continuousMotionCount(diagram)` is the structure for a future motion budget, with no warning shipped (as asked). **Flow** marches the line's dash pattern (its own, or `8 6` on a solid line) one pattern length per cycle; its still form is the dashes standing, drawn by the SVG/PNG/PDF exports from the same helper (`canvas/motionStyle.ts`). **Pulse** glows in the nesting accent with an optional ripple ring; its still form is the shape as authored. **Authoring:** a Motion button in the shape context bar (and between Nested and Arrange for a Nested Block) and a Motion popover in the line toolbar. **Performance, measured on the largest single-level template (fishbone, 41 shapes and 20 lines) with every line flowing and ten shapes pulsing, on the production build:** frames held at the display rate, but the first version kept the main thread busy 1.85 s of every 3 (0.03 s with motion off). Animating a `filter` repainted every frame, so Pulse became a pre-painted glow layer whose opacity alone animates (compositor-only: 1,339 → 83 ms); a dash offset cannot be composited, so Flow steps one pixel at a time — a repaint per pixel of travel instead of per display frame, which looks the same (1,043 → 344 ms); together 1,848 → 643 ms. Only the open level is mounted and culling unmounts what is off screen, so nothing off screen or on another level animates. **Tests:** `model/motion.test.ts` (v6 only when used, byte-identical otherwise, round trip, validation, edits, count), context-bar tests, and `m30-motion.spec.ts` (authoring both, every off switch, the viewer's switch and its absence on a still diagram, `v=6` in the link, the export's still form). **No new dependencies.** |

everything else dims; a hint pill names it, with Stop. **Across levels**, through ports (`nesting/trace.ts`, pure and unit-tested): tracing a line into a Nested Block lights the port it lands on; opening the block carries the trace inside to the shape each lit port maps to (tracing the block itself carries every mapped port), with those shapes' ghost pills lit; a traced shape that is deleted simply stops being lit. **Input:** in the share-link viewer a click traces (the viewer selects nothing, so a click is free for it); in the editor, where a click selects, a Trace button in the context bar (one shape or Nested Block; pressed again, it stops) and a palette command "Trace connections"; a click on empty canvas ends it in both; Esc ends it after the selection is cleared and before a level is left. **Drawn** with classes on React Flow's nodes and edges (`toFlowNode`/`toFlowEdge`), so starting or stopping a trace rebuilds them once and costs nothing per frame; the march is Flow's own, so every motion switch stops it and the line rests as dashes. **Found and fixed on the way:** (1) two ghost pills reaching the same inner shape on the same side were drawn on top of each other ("← Billing · parent arent"); | D118 | 2026-10-07 | **A1b: which way a line flows.** User request: Flow ran one way only. `EdgeMotion.direction` — `forward` (from the line's start to its end, the default and what every earlier line does), `reverse`, or `both` — chosen in the line toolbar's Motion popover beside Speed (→ ← ⇄, named "Along the line", "Reversed", "Both ways"). **Reverse** marches the dashes back toward the start (the same stepped march, its offset's sign flipped). **Both ways** travels four pattern lengths one way and then as far back (`animation-direction: alternate`), which reads as two-way traffic; one pattern there and back read as a jitter, and a second layer marching the other way crossed itself into noise. | D119 | 2026-10-07 | **A3: Packets and Throughput.** Both chosen in the line toolbar's Motion popover beside Flow; speed and direction (A1b) apply to all three and carry over between them. **Packets** — a dot travelling the line — uses SVG's own `<animateMotion>` along the line's path: declarative, no script per frame (CSS motion paths on SVG elements are still unreliable in Safari, as planned). Its pace is constant whatever the line's length (60/120/240 px a second, at least 0.6 s a journey), and direction is its key points — start to end, end to start, or there and back. **CSS cannot pause SMIL**, so each line draws a moving dot and a resting one, and every off switch (reduced motion, the Motion switch, low detail) shows the resting one instead. Its still form is that dot, 60% of the way along in the direction it travels, placed by `pathPointAt` with no DOM, so the canvas and every export agree. **Throughput** carries a 1–5 volume in the line's weight — its own width × 0.95 at 1 up to × 2.35 at 5 — moving or still, and marches Flow's dashes faster the more it carries (1.3× Flow's time at 1, half at 5); its still form is that weight, which is why it reads correctly in a picture. One shared module (`canvas/motionStyle.ts`) gives the canvas and the SVG/PNG/PDF exports the same width, dashes and dot. A traced line (D117) that already moves now keeps its own motion instead of switching to Flow. **Tests:** `motionStyle.test.ts` (weights, paces, packet timing in each direction, the resting point) and m30 (choosing both from the popover, the packet's moving and resting dots under each switch, both kinds' still forms in the SVG export). **No new dependencies.** |
