| D126 | 2026-10-08 | **Animated icons, I3: traffic and network.** Uploading, Load Balancing, Live Traffic, Cache Hit; new still twins Upload and Traffic in Misc; Load Balancing pairs with the network Load Balancer and Cache Hit with the Lightning Bolt. **Live Traffic is a two-sided broadcast, not a fan**: drawn first as three stacked arcs over a dot, it read as the existing Wifi icon at every size, so it radiates both ways from a source instead — the familiar "on air" mark — and stays distinct from Wifi in search results. **Load Balancing's dots rest mid-branch** and travel by plain translate along each straight branch (no motion path needed), staggered so one request is always on its way. **No new dependencies.** |

So the link and its pre-filled subject and body (`app/feedback.ts`) are removed, and **Tell us what's missing** shows `BRAND.contact` as plain text that one click selects whole (`user-select: all`), to paste into whatever mail people use. The dialog now has no links at all, and its Tab loop is Close → ⤢. `docs/about-copy.md`'s note changed first, as before. **No new dependencies.** |

They **match Health's badge** (D120): the same status tokens, a symbol on every disc so colour is never the only signal, and Degraded blinks on the badge's rhythm. The white symbol is a token too (`--nd-icon-on`), so the no-hardcoded-colour test still holds for every body. Healthy's light never dims; only its halo breathes, because a status light that fades reads as failing. **No new dependencies.** |

| D130 | 2026-10-08 | **N10: selection, focus and Pulse follow the shape's own outline.** User report with a screen recording: a selected diamond or triangle showed a rectangular frame (React Flow's resizer line) and, with several selected, a tinted rectangle over the lot; Pulse glowed as a rounded rectangle round any shape, because the glow was a box-shadow on the node's box. Now one component, `ShapeHalo`, draws a copy of the stencil's body with every part stroked in the accent and nothing filled (CSS outranks the body's own paint, so pictograms outline too). It serves as the **selection and a trace's focus** (shown by `.selected` / `.nd-trace-focus`, otherwise transparent), **Pulse's glow** (a wide blurred stroke whose opacity alone animates — painted once, as D116 required) and **the ripple**. The **resizer keeps its handles** (resizing is about the box) but its line is invisible, still draggable; React Flow's **multi-selection rectangle** is drawn clear but still drags the selection. Vendor icons and pictures have no outline of ours and keep a box. draw.io marks a selection with a dashed box; this is the user's call over draw.io's (CLAUDE.md allows Nedito's own visuals, D90). **No new dependencies.** |
| D131 | 2026-10-08 | **G1: Animated GIF export.** User request: "since we are giving motion in diagrams, allow exporting the diagram as a GIF so it can be embedded in sites or social media posts." **Animated image (.gif)** in the Export menu (and the palette), after Image. **Frames are the SVG export at one moment** (`SvgExportOptions.frame`): rather than re-implementing every motion kind for pictures, a frame carries the canvas's own `motion.css` and `animatedIcons.css` (read as text) with every animation paused and delayed by −t — a paused CSS animation with a negative delay draws exactly that moment, in an SVG drawn as an image too (checked in the browser before building on it). Late starters keep their lateness (their delays are read from the same sheets); the off switches are cut, because a picture someone asked for always moves; comments are stripped and the sheet sits in CDATA, since a comment naming `<animateMotion>` broke the markup. What CSS cannot do is computed: Packets and Walkthrough's dot are SMIL on the canvas, so their position at t comes from the same timing functions; Build leaves unrevealed steps out; Spotlight steps the others back. Pulse glows on the shape's own outline, as D130 draws it. A moving icon gets its own 0..100 viewport in a frame, as each canvas glyph has. **Timing**: 12.5 frames a second; continuous motion loops for 4.8s (a whole cycle of every icon); Build runs its reveal and holds; Spotlight shows up to ten steps; Walkthrough one walk; at most 150 frames, a longer loop gets fewer frames a second. Longest side 1200px. A level with no motion makes a one-frame GIF, and the notice says how to add motion. **The encoder is written in-repo** (`gifEncoder.ts`, no new dependency): one median-cut palette for the whole loop, each later frame storing only the rectangle that changed, unchanged frames merged into the previous one's delay, LZW per GIF89a — tested against a decoder written from the spec, including the 4096-code reset. Measured: a small flowing diagram, 60 frames in 1.1s (289 KB); the largest template with all 20 lines flowing, 2.0s (679 KB). Vitest now processes the two motion sheets (it stubs CSS to nothing by default). |
| D132 | 2026-10-08 | **X1: draw.io's cloud icons import as ours.** Audit finding 1 (D112): AWS, Azure and GCP icons arrived as coloured boxes. `file/drawio/cloudIcons.ts` reads the service draw.io names — `resIcon=mxgraph.aws4.*` or `shape=mxgraph.aws4.*` (not its group frames), Azure's `img/lib/azure2/…/X.svg` (or the older `mscae`), GCP's `prIcon=` — and finds Nedito's official icon for that service in the vendor manifests we already ship: **by name** (vendor words dropped, plurals folded), then through **a short alias list** of draw.io's abbreviations (`bucket`/`s3` → Simple Storage Service, `google_kubernetes_engine` → GKE, …), then by **every word of draw.io's name in one of ours** with at most two words over. Matching nothing keeps the old fallback and its notice — a wrong icon would be worse than an honest box. Only draw.io's *names* are read; nothing of its drawing is used (provenance guardrail). A vendor icon takes none of draw.io's colours (it is drawn as the vendor publishes it), only its label placement. It is matched before the picture rule, since draw.io stores Azure icons as linked pictures. The matcher loads with the first draw.io import, not with the app. Finding 11 too: `shape=cloud` → `basic/cloud`. **No new dependencies.** |
| D133 | 2026-10-08 | **X2: draw.io's arrowheads keep their meaning.** Audit finding 2: the importer predated Nedito's UML and crow's-foot heads and collapsed every head to none, open or classic, so composition, aggregation and generalisation lost their meaning and every ER relationship read as an arrow at both ends. Now `diamond`/`diamondThin` → filled or hollow diamond, `block`/`classic` with `endFill=0` → the hollow triangle, and every `ER*` end → its crow's-foot mark (`ERmany`, `ERone`/`ERmandOne`, `ERzeroToOne`, `ERoneToMany`, `ERzeroToMany`), whatever the fill flag says, since draw.io draws those as lines. A head Nedito lacks (`oval`, `dash`, `async`) keeps the old rule — solid, so classic. **No new dependencies.** |
| D134 | 2026-10-08 | **X3: draw.io's layers, hidden and folded content.** Audit findings 3 and 7: every layer was flattened onto one, so a layer the author had hidden (often drafts or internal notes) showed to everyone the file was passed to, and a collapsed container's children spilled out at full size beside its header. Now each draw.io layer — the root's own children, bottom first — becomes a Nedito layer with its **name, visibility and lock**; every shape and line goes on its layer (the first left implicit, as elsewhere). **Hidden stays hidden**, with a notice saying how to show it. **Folded content** goes on a hidden layer of its own, "Folded in draw.io", rather than being dropped: nothing the author made is lost, and nothing they folded away is put in front of a reader; a line to something folded is folded with it, so it never ends in empty space. **No new dependencies.** |
| D135 | 2026-10-08 | **X4: draw.io's line labels, lines on lines, and fixed ends.** Audit findings 4, 8 and 10. **Labels stored as cells of their own** (`edgeLabel` children — draw.io's form for a label dragged along a line, or a second one) no longer arrive as 120×60 boxes near the origin with an "edgelabel" notice: the first becomes the line's label, its place along the line kept as D97's `labelPosition` (draw.io's −1..1 becomes t 0..1; its offset becomes dx/dy), and a line's own label keeps its place the same way. A line here has one label, so a second stays as **text beside the line**, with a notice — kept, not dropped. **A line ending on another line** was skipped; now that end is left loose at the other line's middle (its middle waypoint, or halfway between its ends), with a notice — the connection is drawn. **Fixed ends** (`exitX/exitY`, `entryX/entryY`): the middle of a side becomes that side's handle, any other point a fixed anchor there (`EdgeEnd.anchor`), so lines leave and arrive where the original's did. **No new dependencies.** |
| D136 | 2026-10-08 | **X5: draw.io's tables, swimlanes and containers.** Audit findings 5 and 6. **Tables**: `shape=table` becomes the Entity Table (its name in the band), and its `tableRow`s and `partialRectangle` cells with every side off come across **borderless** — the rows invisible but still there, because relationships attach to them — so a two-column table is no longer eleven stacked outlines. draw.io's entity-relation line style runs side to side from the facing sides, as it does there. **Swimlanes** keep their title band (`advanced/swimlane-vertical`, or the BPMN lane when draw.io turns it with `horizontal=0`). **Containment**: a swimlane, table or `container=1` cell and everything inside it become one Nedito group, so moving it moves its contents — only the outermost (groups are flat here), never one inside or holding a draw.io group, never a collapsed one (its contents are folded away, D134). **`fillColor=none` is now see-through** everywhere, as in draw.io, rather than the stencil's white. Known limit: a table cell narrower than its text plus the canvas's label padding wraps (a 30px "PK" column) — legible, and not worth distorting the table for. **No new dependencies.** |
| D137 | 2026-10-08 | **X6: draw.io's placeholders, links and tooltips — the import audit is done.** Audit findings 9 and 12. An `<object>` or `<UserObject>` with `placeholders="1"` now shows its label as draw.io does, each `%name%` filled from the object's own attributes (an unknown name left as written, `%%` a percent sign) — "Owner: SRE", not "Owner: %owner%". Shapes here carry no links or tooltips, so those are **said, not dropped quietly**: one notice per page names the link (when there is one) and points at the shapes. All six import fixes (X1–X6, D132–D137) have shipped, and the audit's fixtures are the import's regression suite. **No new dependencies.** |
| D138 | 2026-10-08 | **N7: the deletion prompts D12 deferred.** D12 shipped silent cascades because v1 had no dialogs; it has them now. **Deleting a Nested Block that holds shapes** asks: *Keep the contents here* (focused — the safe choice), *Delete everything*, or Cancel. "Keep" lifts the block's shapes and lines onto this level with their top-left where the block's was, above what is already there, keeping their ids (unique across the document) and dropping their old level's layers; Nested Blocks inside keep their own levels; a link that entered through a **mapped** port now goes straight to the shape it led to, one through an unmapped port led nowhere and goes with the block. This replaces CLAUDE.md's "detach to an unused pool": a pool had nowhere to be seen or reattached from (the question raised in Q3, D113), and the user approved whichever was pending (2026-10-08) — lifting is the form of keeping a reader can see. **Deleting a shape a parent's link enters through** asks: *Keep the link waiting* (the port stays, unmapped — D12's amber ghost, relinked by dragging) or *Remove the link too* (the port and the parent's lines on it). An empty block, or a shape no link runs through, deletes at once as before. Esc or Cancel keeps everything; each choice is one undo entry, which the dialog says. A block is named as its box names it (label, else its level's title). **No new dependencies.** |
| D139 | 2026-10-08 | **N11: a narrow box's label keeps its word whole.** Fixes the known limit left in D136 (user request). The canvas padded every label 8px each side, so a draw.io table's 30px key column had 14px for text and broke "PK" into "P" over "K" (the export, which never wraps, was fine). Now one rule, `labelSidePad` in `canvas/labelLayout.ts`, gives 8px on any box 80px wide or more and a tenth of the width below that (2px at least) — 3px in the 30px column, room for the word. The canvas and the export both use it (the export for placing left- and right-aligned text), so they still agree. A general fix rather than an importer special case: any small shape with a short label benefits; boxes 80px and wider are unchanged. **No new dependencies.** |
| D140 | 2026-10-08 | **G2: GIFs at 25 frames a second.** User request: the GIF's 12.5 frames a second (D131) looked jerky. Now 40ms a frame — the smoothest a GIF plays reliably: it stores each frame's time in hundredths of a second, and browsers slow any frame under 2 hundredths to 10, so 4 is the floor worth using. It also catches every step of a Flow line's march (about 20 a second), which 12.5 skipped. The cap rises from 150 to 300 frames, so a normal 4.8s loop keeps the full rate (120 frames); only a long Spotlight drops to fewer frames a second. A slowed frame time is now rounded to whole hundredths, so the loop keeps its length exactly. Measured: a small flowing diagram 2.5s to make, 247 KB; the largest template with all 20 lines flowing 3.8s, 1.05 MB (frame differencing keeps the growth well under double). **No new dependencies.** |
| D141 | 2026-10-08 | **G3: the GIF's size, chosen.** User request, after D140 measured 1 MB for the largest template. "Animated image" in the Export menu now opens a small dialog, in the PowerPoint export's look, with one choice: **Small, Medium or Large** — 600, 900 or 1200px on the longest side, a GIF's file growing with its area — each line naming where it suits (a chat or post; a page or document; full detail) and the pixels this level would come out at, scaled down to fit and never up (so on a small diagram Large is its full size). The choice is remembered per browser (`file/gifSize.ts`), Large until changed, so nothing changes for anyone who does not choose. The button shows progress while the frames are drawn; Esc or Cancel closes it without making anything. **No new dependencies.** |
| D142 | 2026-10-08 | **G4: the GIF's frame rate, chosen too.** User request, after measuring the sizes: the size alone shrinks a big diagram's GIF less than its area suggests (Small ≈ 42% of Large), because a GIF stores only what changes between frames — mostly the moving lines, whose length follows the picture's side, not its area. The other lever is the frame rate, so the dialog gains a **Motion** choice beside Size: **Smooth** (25 frames a second, D140) or **Lighter file** (12.5, the rate before D140). Measured: the largest template with every line flowing, Large 1,054 → 679 KB, Medium 747 → 481, Small 447 → 287 KB; the small three-shape diagram 247 → 132 KB. So the hint says "a third to a half smaller", not "half" — less on a busy diagram, where the first full frame is a larger share of the file. A long loop still stretches either rate to stay under 300 frames. Both choices remembered per browser; Smooth until changed. **No new dependencies.** |
| D143 | 2026-10-08 | **G5: sharper GIFs — Extra large, scaling up, and a streaming encoder.** User report with a screenshot: a Large GIF of the Fishbone template (natural 1395×760, so Large drew it at 0.86×, 1200×654) had soft, ~10px labels. A GIF carries no DPI — sharpness is pixels. So: (1) **Extra large**, 2400px on the longest side — twice Large, for high-resolution screens and slides; on the Fishbone, 2.66 MB Smooth or 1.67 MB Lighter, about 10s / 6s to make. (2) **Every size may now scale a diagram up, to twice its own size**, as the PNG export draws (scale 2) — the drawing is vector, so it stays crisp, and a small diagram no longer comes out identical at every size. (3) **The encoder streams**: frames used to be held, all of them, before encoding — 120 frames at 2400px would be ~1.5 GB. Now the palette comes from five frames spread over the loop, then each frame is compared with the last as raw pixels, only its changed rectangle indexed and compressed, and let go; only the previous frame is kept (`GifStreamEncoder`). The decoder-checked tests cover the streamed path too. **No new dependencies.** |

Entries above are left exactly as written, old paths included. |

| D146 | 2026-10-10 | **Repository security baseline for the public repository.** In the repository's settings: `main` protected (changes only by pull request, no force pushes or deletion, admins bound too); Dependabot alerts and security updates on; private vulnerability reporting on; secret scanning and push protection on; GitHub Actions limited to GitHub's own actions, the workflow token read-only, and workflows from outside contributors' pull requests held for approval; the unused wiki and projects switched off. In the files: `SECURITY.md` (report through GitHub's private reporting, so no address is published outside `BRAND.contact`), `.github/dependabot.yml` (one grouped version-update pull request per ecosystem), and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the verification sweep on Windows, actions pinned to commit SHAs, credentials not kept after checkout). `npm audit fix` updated the lockfile for the build and lint tools' advisories; Vitest's (`tinypool`, `@vitest/mocker`) need a major upgrade and are in the Inbox. None of it reaches the shipped app: `npm audit --omit=dev` reports nothing. | A public repository is open to anyone's pull requests, issues and scans. These are GitHub's recommended defaults for one, and running the sweep in CI makes it a check GitHub can require before a merge rather than a promise in CLAUDE.md. |
| D147 | 2026-10-10 | **Vitest 2.1 → 4.1.** The test runner moved up two majors; nothing else in the stack changed (Vite 6 and jsdom were already supported). Two adjustments: Vitest 4 reports a module still loading after a test file's environment is torn down, which Vitest 2 ignored, and four files that render the shape library or the app (`App`, `AppShell`, `ErrorBoundary`, `ShapeLibrary`) left the lazy stencil-section and template loads running, so they now wait for them in an `afterAll` (`src/testLazyLoads.ts`, test-only); and `vi.fn()`'s type is stricter, so `download.test.ts` types its click mock. Same 1,463 unit tests, all passing, no app code changed. | Vitest 2.1 carried the `tinypool` (critical) and `@vitest/mocker` (moderate) advisories that D146 left in the Inbox, and the fix exists only in the new major; `npm audit` now reports nothing, development tools included. |
| D148 | 2026-10-10 | **The Cloudflare Worker is named `nedito`.** `wrangler.jsonc`'s `name` changed from `deepdraw`, reversing that part of D100. Author's decision. After the move to the public repository, Workers Builds' preview uploads (`wrangler versions upload`, run for every non-production branch) failed with "the name in your wrangler.jsonc file (deepdraw) must match the name of your Worker", while production deploys from `main` succeeded. Consequence, recorded as D100 warned: a deploy under the new name goes to the Worker called `nedito`, so the custom domains (nedito.app, www.nedito.app) and the Workers Builds connection must be on that Worker in the Cloudflare dashboard for the site to keep updating. | Author's call, to make the Worker's name match the product and the build connection. |

Parity is now guarded by tests that were checked to **fail** when each bug is reintroduced (2 E2E + 4 unit). ## Completed work

### Pre-repo (design phase, claude.ai sessions)
- 2026-08-04 — Product concept defined: draw.io parity + nested diagrams differentiator
- 2026-08-04 — Interactive UX mock built (nested-diagram-mock.html): 3-level drill-down, breadcrumbs, ports, ghost links, auto-fit canvas; validated interaction design
- 2026-08-04 — Data model v1 drafted (flat diagram map, NestedNode + ports, EdgeEnd with optional portId); adopted into CLAUDE.md
- 2026-08-04 — CLAUDE.md, PROGRESS.md, CLAUDE_HISTORY.md authored

### M0 — Scaffold
- 2026-08-04 — Vite + React 18 + TS strict scaffold, ESLint 9 flat config + Prettier, Vitest + RTL wired; `npm run dev/test/build/lint` all pass clean (M0 exit test)
- 2026-08-04 — `src/app/brand.ts` with `BRAND` object (name, fileExt, formatId, storagePrefix) per brand indirection section
- 2026-08-04 — `tokens.css` (canvas/ink/nesting-accent/topbar tokens) + app shell: top bar (menus + breadcrumb), left shape-library sidebar (section list placeholder), right format-panel sidebar, canvas region, bottom page-tab strip
- 2026-08-04 — React Flow canvas: dot-grid background, space/middle-drag pan (`useSpaceDragPan`), Ctrl+scroll zoom via `panOnScroll`+`zoomOnScroll=false`, 10%–400% zoom range, `Controls` fit-view button, live zoom % badge
- 2026-08-04 — Zustand store skeleton (`useDocStore`): `{ doc, navStack, selection, viewportByDiagramId, ui }`; `createInitialState` seeds one empty root diagram
- 2026-08-04 — `src/model/types.ts` (full data model from CLAUDE.md, plus `StyleMap`/`RichLabel`/`EdgeEnd`/`GroupNode` which CLAUDE.md references but doesn't define inline) and `src/model/validate.ts` with invariant checks (duplicate ids, missing root/child diagrams, nesting cycles via DFS coloring, dangling ports) + 9 unit tests
- Dependencies added beyond the CLAUDE.md-mandated stack: `@eslint/js`, `globals` (ESLint 9 flat-config requires these directly; not bundled), `@types/node` (needed for `node:path`/`__dirname` in vite/vitest configs). Also added a `package.json` `overrides.vite` pin — vitest 2.1.9 was nesting its own vite 5.4.21 alongside our vite 6.4.3, which broke `vitest/config`'s `defineConfig` typing against the `@vitejs/plugin-react` plugin object; the override forces one deduped vite version tree-wide.
- Known issue (not fixed): `npm audit` flags a critical advisory in vitest &lt;3.2.6 (arbitrary file read/execute when the Vitest UI server is listening). We never pass `--ui`, so it isn't reachable via `npm run test`/`test:watch`, but a major-version bump to vitest 4 would be needed to clear it — deferred as a deliberate choice pending user sign-off on the breaking change.

### M1 — Shapes on canvas
- 2026-08-04 — Stencil architecture: `StencilDef`/`StencilSection` types, lazy per-section loader (`loadSection`) with only General implemented (rect, rounded rect, ellipse, diamond, text, note — original geometry, no drawio-derived assets); `StencilGlyph` renders the shared SVG body via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` (safe: content is static, repo-authored markup, never user input); sidebar supports both drag-to-canvas and click-to-place (center of current viewport)
- 2026-08-04 — Command-pattern actions (`src/model/actions.ts`): pure `addNode`/`removeNodes`/`updateNodes`/`setNodeLabel`/`bringToFront`/`duplicateNodes` doc-transform functions, unit tested in isolation from the store
- 2026-08-04 — `zundo` wired into `useDocStore` with `partialize: (state) => ({ doc: state.doc })` — only the model enters undo history; selection/viewport/ui/clipboard/editingNodeId churn never does. `useTemporalStore` hook exposes the temporal store to components
- 2026-08-04 — `ShapeNode` custom React Flow node: renders the stencil SVG themed via `node.style.fill`/`stroke`, `NodeResizer` for the 8-handle resize UI, a custom `RotateHandle` (plain `window` pointermove/pointerup listeners — deliberately not using React Flow's d3-drag machinery) for rotation, inline `LabelEditor` overlay (double-click/F2) with a floating mini toolbar for bold/italic/font size/color/align — matches the `RichLabel` model (whole-label styling, not per-run rich text)
- 2026-08-04 — Selection: click/shift-click/rubber-band via React Flow's built-in `selectionOnDrag` + `multiSelectionKeyCode="Shift"`, mirrored into `store.selection` via `onSelectionChange`; grid snap toggle (`ui.snapEnabled`) wired to `snapToGrid`/`snapGrid`; arrow-key nudge (1px, 10px/`GRID_SIZE` with Shift) in `useGlobalShortcuts`
- 2026-08-04 — Global keyboard shortcuts (`src/app/useGlobalShortcuts.ts`): Ctrl+Z/Y undo/redo, Ctrl+C/V/D copy/paste/duplicate, Delete/Backspace, F2 edit, Esc cancel-or-deselect, arrow nudge; skips when focus is in a form field. The label editor stops propagation on every keydown while active (capture-phase, see below), so native text-field behavior (including browser undo) works unobstructed while editing
- 2026-08-04 — **Known jsdom/testing gotcha, documented for future test-writing**: React Flow's node dragging uses d3-drag, which reads `view(node).document` and throws when `ownerDocument.defaultView` isn't resolvable the way jsdom + `userEvent`'s full mousedown/mouseup replay produce it. Fix used in tests: `fireEvent.doubleClick` instead of `userEvent.dblClick` for node interactions. Separately, in the app itself the label editor overlay must stop event propagation in the **capture phase** (`onMouseDownCapture`/`onPointerDownCapture`), not the bubble phase — React's delegated listeners fire at the root container, so a bubble-phase `stopPropagation()` inside the overlay runs *after* the native event already reached React Flow's d3-drag listener on the ancestor node
- 2026-08-04 — Verified live in-browser (real Chromium via the dev server, not just jsdom): click-to-place for all 6 General shapes with correct sizes, 8 resize handles + rotate handle appear on selection, rotate-handle drag commits rotation, double-click label editing commits text, Ctrl+D/Delete/Ctrl+C+Ctrl+V, and Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y undo/redo across all of the above — zero console errors. Node drag-move and NodeResizer-handle drag were **not** live-verified (synthetic `PointerEvent`/`dispatchEvent` can't replicate native browser pointer capture that d3-drag depends on) — both commit through the same `moveOrResizeNodes` action that the rotate-handle test and `actions.test.ts` already cover, and neither is custom drag logic (React Flow's own dragging/`NodeResizer` implementation), so residual risk is low but not zero. Worth a manual mouse-drag smoke test the next time a human has hands on the app.
- Dependency added: `@testing-library/user-event` (M1 exit test note: realistic click/type/keyboard simulation for interaction tests, standard RTL companion, not previously needed when M0 only had static-render tests)

### M2 — Edges
- 2026-08-05 — Edge model actions (`addEdge`/`removeEdges`/`updateEdge`, plus a combined `removeSelection` replacing the old node-only `removeNodes` so a mixed node+edge Delete press is one undo entry) and z-order (`sendToBack`/`moveForward`/`moveBackward`, swapping z with the immediate neighbor from a pre-swap snapshot) and align/distribute (`alignNodes`, `distributeNodes` — equal-gap spacing) pure functions, all unit tested
- 2026-08-05 — `ConnectionHandles`: 8 hover-visible handles per shape (4 directional arrows at midpoints + 4 corner dots), all declared `type="source"` with `connectionMode="loose"` on `<ReactFlow>` so any handle can complete either end of a connection; hidden while the node is selected so they don't visually collide with `NodeResizer`'s handles at the same 8 positions
- 2026-08-05 — `DiagramEdge` custom edge: straight/orthogonal/curved rendering (RF's `getStraightPath`/`getSmoothStepPath`/`getBezierPath` when no waypoints exist, hand-rolled polyline builders in `edgeGeometry.ts` — axis-aligned elbow-per-segment for orthogonal, quadratic-smoothed for curved — when waypoints exist), configurable arrowheads (`MarkerType.Arrow`/`ArrowClosed` mapped from `style.startArrow`/`endArrow`) and dash patterns
- 2026-08-05 — Waypoint editing: dragging anywhere on an edge line inserts a new waypoint at the nearest segment (`nearestSegmentIndex`) past a small movement threshold (distinguishes drag-to-add from click/double-click-to-edit-label, which would otherwise fire on the same pointerdown); dragging an existing waypoint handle moves it; double-clicking a waypoint removes it. Same live-preview-then-commit-on-release pattern as node drag/rotate (local `dragState`, one store commit on pointerup = one undo entry)
- 2026-08-05 — Edge labels reuse the M1 `LabelEditor` overlay via `EdgeLabelRenderer`, positioned at the centroid of source+waypoints+target; background pill when not editing
- 2026-08-05 — Alignment guides: `computeSnap` (in `alignmentSnap.ts`) checks the dragged node's edges/center against every other node's edges/center within a 6px threshold during `onNodeDrag`, snaps by overriding the local RF node position via `setNodes`, and `AlignmentGuides` renders the matched guide line in screen space (not the flow-transformed layer, so it stays hairline-thin at any zoom). `SelectionToolbar` is a minimal floating toolbar (align L/C/R/T/M/B always, distribute H/V at 3+ selected) — intentionally lightweight; the real Arrange-tab surface for these same store actions is M5's Format Panel work
- 2026-08-05 — **Root-caused a real bug, not a red herring**: edges added programmatically (including via the interactive connect-by-drag flow) never rendered — `getInternalNode(id).internals.handleBounds` stayed `undefined` forever. Traced through `@xyflow/system` source to `useResizeObserver`/`updateNodeInternals`: handle-bounds measurement is scheduled via `requestAnimationFrame`, and rAF is unconditionally suspended by Chromium for hidden/non-visible tabs — which the Browser pane tab was, from this tool's vantage point, for most of this debugging session (`document.hidden` was `true`; confirmed by testing a bare `requestAnimationFrame` callback that never fired). Not fixable from application code; resolved by having the user bring the pane into an actually-visible state, after which everything worked immediately with no code changes needed beyond a defensive `useUpdateNodeInternals()` call (kept — matches React Flow's own documented guidance for custom nodes with a static handle set, harmless, and correct for real users on a normal visible tab regardless of this tooling-specific cause)
- 2026-08-05 — Also added a permanent `onError` handler on `<ReactFlow>` (logs to console) and a `window.__ddStore` / `window.__rf` dev-only debug hook (gated by `import.meta.env.DEV`, stripped from production builds) — both came out of this investigation and are worth keeping for future debugging
- 2026-08-05 — jsdom needs a `DOMMatrixReadOnly` stub too (added to `setupTests.ts` alongside the M0 `ResizeObserver` stub) — `useUpdateNodeInternals`'s rAF-scheduled work reads `.m22` off one to derive zoom; a fixed identity-scale stub is sufficient since tests don't exercise real zoomed transforms
- 2026-08-05 — Verified live in-browser (real Chromium, pane visible): all three edge styles render with correct distinct path geometry and arrowheads; dragging an orthogonal edge's line adds a waypoint and reroutes it with a visible right-angle detour (confirmed both via DOM `d` attribute and screenshot); double-click edge label editing commits text; a clean isolated sequence (connect → add waypoint → set label → undo×3 fully unwinds to edge deletion → redo×3 fully restores) confirms undo/redo is coherent across all of it. Node drag-move remains unverifiable via this tooling (same native-pointer-capture gap noted in the M1 entry) regardless of tab visibility — confirmed by retesting it once the pane was visible and it still didn't move, isolating that as a separate, distinct limitation from the rAF one

### M3 — Nesting core (flagship)
- 2026-08-05 — Nesting model layer (`src/model/nesting.ts`): `addNestedNode` (new empty child diagram + referencing node), `convertSelectionToNested` (moves selected nodes + their internal edges into a new child diagram with positions rebased to `CHILD_MARGIN`, auto-creates one port per boundary-crossing edge — incoming get `side:"left"`, outgoing `side:"right"`, matching the ghost-link convention — and rewrites those parent edges to target the new nested node's port), port CRUD (`addPort`/`updatePort`/`removePort`, the latter clearing `portId` off any edge that referenced it), `collectDescendantDiagramIds` + `cascadePortCleanup` + `removeSelectionCascading` (D12), and `findDiagramPath` (BFS root→target through nested nodes, used by both breadcrumb navigation and cross-level undo relocation). All pure, all unit tested (18 tests)
- 2026-08-05 — Store navigation (`navigateInto`/`navigateBack`/`navigateToLevel`/`navigateToDiagram`, `saveViewport`) plus nesting mutation wrappers, all clearing selection/editing state on any nav-stack change since node/edge ids from one diagram are meaningless in another
- 2026-08-05 — Cross-level undo/redo (`src/model/undoNavigation.ts`): `findPrimaryChangedDiagramId` diffs the doc's `diagrams` map before/after an undo or redo, preferring a diagram that exists in both but changed (edited in place — e.g. the parent when a convert-to-nested is undone) over one purely added/removed (e.g. the freshly-created child), then relocates the nav stack there via `findDiagramPath` if it differs from where the user currently is. `useGlobalShortcuts`'s Ctrl+Z/Y now route through `undoWithNavigation`/`redoWithNavigation` instead of calling the temporal store directly
- 2026-08-05 — `NestedNode` custom node: stacked-card visual (two offset ghost `<div>`s behind the body, per the CLAUDE.md visual identity section), layers badge + ⤢ open button, collapsed icon row + summary, `NodeResizer` (no rotate handle — a rotated container with side-anchored ports would be a mess, a deliberate v1 scope cut), reuses `ConnectionHandles` from M2 for generic connections plus one `PortHandle` per port (id `port:<portId>`, positioned by `side`/`offset`) so edges can attach to a specific port
- 2026-08-05 — `navigation.ts` orchestrates the animated zoom transition entirely through React Flow's own duration-based `fitView`/`setViewport` (no custom animation code): `navigateIntoNested` first fits the viewport to the nested node's box on the diagram being left (a "diving in" lead-in), then swaps `navStack` once that settles; every navigation path saves the outgoing diagram's viewport first, and a `diagramId`-keyed effect in `DiagramCanvas` restores a saved viewport or falls back to `fitView()` on first visit
- 2026-08-05 — "Nested diagram" stencil (`general/nested`) is special-cased in `ShapeLibrary` and `DiagramCanvas`'s `onDrop` (a `kind: "nested"` flag in the drag payload) to call `addNestedNode` instead of `addShapeNode` — placing it creates the node *and* an empty child diagram in one action
- 2026-08-05 — Minimal right-click node context menu (`NodeContextMenu`) with exactly one item, "Convert to nested diagram" — a deliberately small seed of the full context-menu system M5 owns, added because M3 has no other trigger for a CLAUDE.md-mandated action ("select shapes → right-click → Convert to nested diagram")
- 2026-08-05 — Format panel "Nested" section (`NestedFormatSection`): summary text (commit on blur), up to 4 collapsed icons (add from any cached stencil, remove via ×), port list with inline label editing and an add-port row (side + inner-node picker sourced from the child diagram's actual nodes) — this fully replaces the generic Style/Text/Arrange placeholder for a single-nested-node selection until M5 builds the real tabbed panel
- 2026-08-05 — Ghost links (`ghostLinkGeometry.ts` + `GhostLinks` overlay): pure `computeGhostLinks(doc, navStack)` finds the parent diagram's nested node pointing at the current diagram, matches its ports against parent edges by `portId`, and returns incoming/outgoing links with the *other* endpoint's label; the overlay renders them as dashed pills in screen space (not the flow-transformed layer, so they stay pinned near the canvas edge regardless of zoom) at the Y position of the mapped inner node — `⇠ {label} · parent` on the left for incoming, `{label} · parent ⇢` on the right for outgoing, exactly matching the CLAUDE.md example strings
- 2026-08-05 — **Real bug found and fixed via live browser testing, not a tooling red herring this time**: `store.connect()` (written in M2, before ports existed) only ever wrote raw handle-id strings into `style.sourceHandle`/`targetHandle` and never populated `EdgeEnd.portId`, so ghost links (which key off `portId`) silently found nothing even though the port-handle connection itself worked visually. Fixed with a `parsePortId()` helper that recognizes the `port:<id>` handle-id convention `PortHandle` uses and sets it on the edge end; regression test added in `store.test.ts`
- 2026-08-05 — Verified live in-browser (real Chromium, pane visible), built the full checkout scenario end-to-end: API Gateway → nested Payment Service (2 ports) → Orders DB at the root; drilled into Payment Service and confirmed both ghost-link pills render with the exact CLAUDE.md wording; drilled further into a nested Fraud Engine and confirmed the 3-level breadcrumb (`Page 1 › Payment Service › Fraud Engine`); breadcrumb click and Esc both navigate correctly; and — the one genuinely hard-to-fake part — pressing Ctrl+Z from 3 levels deep correctly undid a root-level edge connect *and* relocated the nav stack all the way back to root, with Ctrl+Y restoring both the edge and (implicitly) staying put since the redo's effect was already visible. `convertSelectionToNested` also re-verified end-to-end via the store, separately from its context-menu trigger
- Two Windows-filesystem case-collision near-misses this milestone, both caught by `tsc` immediately: `GhostLinks.tsx` vs `ghostLinks.ts` (renamed the pure module to `ghostLinkGeometry.ts`) — worth remembering before naming a new component file after an existing lowercase-first utility module of the same feature name, or vice versa

### M4 — Document & files
- 2026-08-05 — Multi-page: pure `src/model/pages.ts` (`addPage`/`removePage`/`reorderPages`, the latter refusing to drop the last remaining page and cascading `collectDescendantDiagramIds` cleanup into `removePage` so a page's nested-diagram subtree doesn't leak into the doc) + store wrappers (`addPage`/`renamePage`/`removePage`/`reorderPages`/`switchPage`) + a fully functional `PageTabStrip` (click to switch, double-click to rename, drag to reorder, hover-delete, + to add)
- 2026-08-05 — `.deepdraw` format module (`src/file/deepdrawFormat.ts`): `encodeDoc` emits `format`/`formatVersion` first (self-identifying header) and sorts the `diagrams` map by id for diff-friendly output; `decodeDoc` never throws — parses, checks format id against a `KNOWN_FORMAT_IDS` registry, checks version isn't newer than supported, runs it through an (currently empty, v1-only) `migrate()` registry, then validates against the existing model invariants (`validate.ts`) before accepting. `docs/FORMAT.md` documents the schema per version
- 2026-08-05 — Hand-rolled SVG export (`src/file/svgExport.ts`, no new dependency): reuses the same polyline builders (`buildStraightPolyline`/`buildOrthogonalPolyline`/`buildSmoothPolyline`) and stencil lookup the live canvas uses, so exported geometry matches on-screen rendering; nested nodes export their author-designed collapsed view (icons + summary), not their (unrelated, separately-diagrammed) children. PNG export (`pngExport.ts`) rasterizes that same SVG through an offscreen `Image`→`<canvas>`→`toBlob` pipeline with a `scale` option for higher-DPI output
- 2026-08-05 — localStorage autosave: `useAutosave` debounces (800ms) writes of the whole doc keyed `${BRAND.storagePrefix}:autosave`; `RestorePrompt` shows CLAUDE.md's exact "Unsaved diagram found — restore?" banner on mount if a saved doc exists, wired into `AppShell`
- 2026-08-05 — File menu (`FileMenu.tsx`): New (confirms before discarding via `loadDoc(createEmptyDoc())`), Open (hidden file input → `file.text()` → `decodeDoc` → `loadDoc`, or `window.alert` on failure), Save (`encodeDoc` → download), Export as PNG/SVG. `useFileDrop` mirrors the Open flow for whole-window drag-and-drop of a `.deepdraw` file. `store.loadDoc` resets `navStack`/`selection`/`viewportByDiagramId`/clipboard and clears undo history — a freshly loaded doc's history would be meaningless
- 2026-08-05 — Diagram settings panel (`DiagramSettings.tsx`, replaces the M0-era placeholder text in `FormatPanel`'s empty-selection view): grid/snap checkboxes bound to the pre-existing-but-previously-unwired `ui.gridEnabled`/`ui.snapEnabled` store flags, plus a background color picker + Reset button driving the new `Diagram.background` field (D13). **Bug found and fixed in the same pass**: `ui.gridEnabled` had existed since M0 with a working `toggleGrid` action, but `DiagramCanvas`'s dot-grid `<Background>` was unconditionally rendered — the toggle changed state but nothing visible ever happened. Now gated on `gridEnabled`
- 2026-08-05 — **jsdom test-environment gap, third of its kind this project** (after M0's `ResizeObserver` and M2's `DOMMatrixReadOnly`): jsdom's `File`/`Blob` has no working `.text()`, needed by the Open-file flow's tests. First attempt polyfilled via Node's `Response` (`new Response(blob).text()`), which passed in isolation but returned the literal string `"[object File]"` for real jsdom `File` instances — Node's `Response` doesn't recognize jsdom's `Blob` as blob-like (different realm/class) and falls back to `String(body)`. Fixed by routing through jsdom's own `FileReader` instead (`setupTests.ts`), which does understand its own `Blob`/`File`
- 2026-08-05 — Verified live in-browser (real Chromium, pane visible): built a multi-page doc with a nested diagram (2 shapes converted to nested, inline "2 shapes" collapsed summary rendering correctly with the stacked-card visual) plus a second page with its own custom background color; confirmed `encodeDoc`→`decodeDoc`→`encodeDoc` is byte-stable on the live doc; confirmed SVG and PNG export render the nested node's collapsed view and a plain shape correctly (screenshot-verified pixel output, not just blob presence); confirmed the autosave→reload→"Unsaved diagram found — restore?"→Restore flow brings back both pages, the nested node, and the per-page background exactly as left; confirmed grid-toggle now actually hides/shows the dot grid and background Reset reverts to the default canvas color. Zero errors from application code (a few `@xyflow/react`-internal console messages persisted from before this milestone's changes and are unrelated to them)
- All 140 tests / 17 files passing, lint clean, `tsc -b` clean at milestone close (up from 128 tests at M3 close)

### M5 — Parity polish (closes Phase 1)
- 2026-08-06 — Format panel Style/Text/Arrange tabs (`StyleTab`/`TextTab`/`ArrangeTab`, replacing the M1-M4 placeholders): Style edits fill/line-color/line-width/opacity for shapes and line-style/color/width/dash/arrowheads for edges; Text bulk-applies bold/italic/size/color/align across a multi-selection; Arrange edits position/size/rotation for a single shape plus z-order and align/distribute always. Added `setNodeStyle`/`setNodeLabelStyle` store actions and generalized `setEdgeStyle` to bulk-apply across a selection in one undo entry. Wired the new style keys (`strokeWidth`/`opacity` on nodes, `stroke`/`strokeWidth` on edges, full label formatting) into both the live canvas renderers and SVG export so editing, on-screen rendering, and exported files all agree
- 2026-08-06 — Flat groups (Ctrl+G / Ctrl+Shift+U, distinct from nesting): a `GroupNode` bounding-box frame renders via React Flow's native parent/child node relationship (`parentId` + `extent:"parent"`) so dragging the frame moves children for free, while the model keeps every node's position absolute per CLAUDE.md's data model — relative coordinates exist only at the `DiagramCanvas` render-adapter boundary (D15). Deleting a grouped child cascades cleanup (strips it from the group, drops the group below 2 children). v1 scope cuts — no resize handle, no click-selects-whole-group redirect, no nested groups — documented in D16
- 2026-08-06 — Full keyboard shortcut map: filled the remaining gaps (Ctrl+A select all, Ctrl+X cut, Ctrl+=/Ctrl+- zoom, Ctrl+Shift+H fit page) alongside the pre-existing set, plus a Ctrl+Shift+K cheat-sheet dialog (also reachable from the Help menu) that swallows other shortcuts while open and closes on Escape/backdrop/close-button. `zoomIn`/`zoomOut` added to `canvasApi.ts` so the shortcut hook, which lives outside the ReactFlowProvider tree, can reach them the same way it already reached `fitView`
- 2026-08-06 — General-purpose `ContextMenu` (replacing M3's single-item `NodeContextMenu`, moved from `src/nesting` to `src/canvas`): adapts to what was right-clicked — empty canvas gets Paste/Select All; a shape or edge selection gets Cut/Copy/Paste/Duplicate/Delete; shapes additionally get z-order, Group (2+ nodes) / Ungroup (a selected group), Convert to nested diagram, or Open child diagram for a single nested node. Right-clicking an unselected edge now selects it first, extending the existing node behavior
- 2026-08-06 — Sidebar search (filters shape labels across enabled sections into a flat results grid) and a "More Shapes…" dialog to enable/disable whole sections, persisted in `localStorage`. Added Flowchart (process/decision/terminator/document/predefined-process/data — standard ISO/ANSI symbols) and Network (server/database/cloud/router/firewall/switch) sections with original generic shapes. Azure/AWS/GCP ship a small set of deliberately generic placeholder "service card" shapes rather than reproductions of the real vendor icon packs — trademark/visual-similarity risk, not something to fabricate from memory inside an automated session (D17); real licensed packs are tracked in the Inbox as human follow-up work
- 2026-08-06 — Scratchpad section: right-click a shape → "Add to Scratchpad" captures a reusable snapshot (shapeRef/size/style/label) in `localStorage`, independent of the `.deepdraw` document. Click-to-place and drag-out-to-canvas both preserve the captured style. "Reuse" is captured via the context menu rather than literal canvas-to-sidebar HTML5 drag-and-drop, since a native `draggable` attribute on a React-Flow-managed node risks conflicting with RF's own pointer-capture drag machinery — the same class of fragility already documented for d3-drag in the M1 entry (D18)
- 2026-08-07 — Performance pass verified live at 500 nodes / 100 edges: enabled `<ReactFlow onlyRenderVisibleElements>` (panning at a realistic zoom level, most nodes off-screen, now matches the ~165fps empty-diagram baseline instead of reconciling all mounted nodes on every viewport change); the belt-and-suspenders `updateNodeInternals` effect now depends on the committed `diagram` doc instead of React Flow's live-drag `nodes` state, so it no longer forces a full handle-bounds remeasure on every intermediate drag frame; `DiagramEdge` wrapped in `memo()` to match the other custom renderers (D19)
- 2026-08-07 — Playwright E2E suite (`playwright.config.ts`, `tests/e2e/`, `npm run test:e2e`) automates the M1-M4 exit-test scenarios previously only verified manually: shapes/selection/labels/undo-redo plus a real mouse drag, edges/waypoints/labels/undo, the M3 checkout scenario (auto-ported nesting, ghost links, breadcrumb nav, cross-level undo relocating the view), and M4's `.deepdraw` save/export/autosave round-trips via real browser downloads. Edge/nesting test *setup* goes through `window.__ddStore` directly rather than driving the hover-to-reveal-handle drag gesture, the single most fragile interaction path during M2 development (D20). 17/17 pass; `npm run test` (Vitest) now excludes `tests/e2e/**`
- 2026-08-07 — Verified live in-browser (real Chromium, pane visible), one combined session exercising every M5 feature together rather than in isolation: placed and connected two flowchart shapes, styled one via the Style tab (fill color) and Text tab (bold), grouped both (Ctrl+G) and confirmed the right-click menu correctly offered Ungroup instead of Convert-to-nested for the group and correctly targeted the individual child (not the group) when right-clicking a shape inside it; captured that child to the Scratchpad, opened the Ctrl+Shift+K cheat sheet, added a nested diagram, drilled in, placed the *same* scratchpad item inside the nested diagram (style preserved), navigated back via breadcrumb, added a second page, confirmed `encodeDoc`→`decodeDoc`→`encodeDoc` byte-stability on the full combined doc (2 pages, 3 diagrams, group+nested+shape node mix), then reloaded and confirmed the restore prompt brought back pixel-identical state. Zero errors from application code
- All 212 Vitest tests / 25 files + 17/17 Playwright E2E tests passing, lint clean, `tsc -b` clean, production build clean at milestone close (up from 140 tests at M4 close). **Phase 1 is now closed** — every CLAUDE.md "Core editor behaviors" checklist item and M0-M5 are complete

Advanced was reviewed against the same complaint and deliberately left alone — its symbols are correct, real ANSI notation, not a defect.
- [x] **Icon library sweep, slice 2**: Misc/Arrows/Network/Business pictograms re-sourced at Material Symbols weight 200 (were weight 400 — the default, but visually heavy compared to the rest of the app); default Style > Line width for newly-placed shapes changed 2 → 1 — done 2026-08-14, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D39.
- [x] **Icon library sweep, slice 3**: sidebar/Favorites/nested-icon-chip previews for every hand-drawn shape (General, Basic, Advanced, Flowchart, UML, Entity Relation, Network's own 6 shapes) now render at a bolder preview-only line weight, decoupled from the canvas/Style-panel default (which stays 1) — done 2026-08-14, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D40. Reached `main` via the recovery PR #12, not its original PR — see the process-incident note below.
- **Process incident (2026-08-14)**: PR #11 was merged capturing only its first commit; the second (D40 above) never reached `main` — same class of incident as D35. Caught immediately via the D35-mandated `git merge-base --is-ancestor` check, recovered via the same playbook (new branch from the orphaned commit, rebased onto current `main`), merged as PR #12.
- [x] **Icon library sweep, slice 4 — scoped then built** (2026-09-15, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D54). The placeholder above was audited before any code changed; findings and disposition:
  - **Audit method**: every section dumped by kind (Material Symbols pictogram vs hand-drawn outline), then labels compared against their stencil ids and glyph path data compared for byte-equality.
  - **Confirmed sound, left alone**: General/Basic/Advanced/Flowchart/UML/Entity Relation are 100% hand-drawn notation geometry, already fixed by slices 1 and 3. Misc/Arrows/Business are 100% Material Symbols at weight 200 per slice 2. Network is the one mixed section (6 hand-drawn originals + 46 pictograms), which slice 3's preview-weight decoupling already accounts for. General/Basic overlap (Square, Circle, Hexagon, Cylinder, Cross…) was checked against draw.io, which overlaps the same way — not a defect, not touched.
  - **Fixed — all 169 pictogram labels were Google's internal Material Symbols id, title-cased.** Most read fine; ~99 did not and were renamed to what the glyph actually draws. Worst offenders: `filter-drama` → Cloud, `emoji-events` → Trophy, `local-fire-department` → Flame, `account-balance` → Bank, `local-shipping` → Delivery Truck, `account-tree` → Org Chart. The intent already existed but was applied inconsistently — DNS/LAN/USB/VPN/SD Card had been hand-corrected, `Nfc` and `Tv` had not.
  - **Fixed — version suffixes leaked**: "Inventory 2" → Inventory, "Diversity 3" → Diversity.
  - **Fixed — vendor product names in labels**: Phone Iphone / Laptop Mac / Tablet Mac / Desktop Windows → Mobile Phone / Laptop / Tablet / Desktop. A product name implies the shape *is* that product, which a generic device glyph does not mean and which this project should not claim in its own UI.
  - **Fixed — four byte-identical duplicate glyphs**, each shipped twice under two of Google's own names: `misc/grade`==`misc/star`, `misc/new-releases`==`misc/verified`, `arrows/call-made`==`arrows/north-east`, `network/backup`==`network/cloud-upload`. Withdrawn from the picker and **aliased**, not deleted — see the hazard below.
  - **Fixed — solid/outline label collisions**: Misc's filled Diamond/Hexagon/Pentagon shared a name with General/Basic's outline versions that hold text. Now "Diamond (Solid)" etc.
  - **Fixed — search only ever matched the label**, so one icon was reachable by exactly one word. Search now also matches the stencil id's own words and an optional `keywords` array. The id match is what keeps every renamed shape findable under the Material Symbols name it used to carry, at zero authoring cost.
  - **Hazard found and contained**: a `shapeRef` that no longer resolves does **not** fail loudly — `canvas/ShapeNode.tsx` falls back to a plain white box, so retiring a stencil id silently strips the picture out of already-saved diagrams. Retirements now go through `RETIRED_STENCIL_IDS` in `library/stencils/index.ts`. Verified live: a node on `misc/grade` still draws its star, while a genuinely unknown ref beside it draws the blank box.
  - **Deliberately still out of scope**: net-new sections and custom/importable shape libraries. That is a feature, not a sweep — it is now its own Inbox item below rather than hiding inside this one.


**First-visit onboarding & local-only disclosure** — four independent items from `docs/archive/onboarding-spec.md`, shipped in this order, one PR at a time. All UI-layer: no document-model or `formatVersion` changes. Copy must never claim the data is "secure", "encrypted", or "private by design" — the accurate claim is that nothing is uploaded.

- [x] **Onboarding 1 — status-bar "Local only" badge + policy popover** — done 2026-08-26, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D47: permanent disclosure pill at the left end of the bottom strip, opening a popover that explains nothing is uploaded, warns that clearing browser data erases the work, and offers a "Download a copy now" button wired to the existing save action. No blocking modal, per spec. **Spec deviation to note:** the spec places the badge "before the zoom/shape-count text" in "the existing status bar" — there is no status bar and no shape-count text in this app (zoom is a canvas overlay), so it went at the left end of the page-tab strip, the only always-present chrome below the canvas.
- [x] **Onboarding 2+3 — first-visit flow (sample diagram + coach marks)** — done 2026-08-26, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D48. Shipped as one item after the sample-only version was rejected in review ("not what we needed") against `first-visit-flow-mock.html`: the introduction only works as a whole. Includes a real **status bar** (local-only badge + live zoom/diagram), the sample with its strip, and the two coach marks with the looping nested-box animation and completion toast.
- [x] **IBM Plex Sans as the application typeface** — done 2026-08-26, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D49: `@fontsource/ibm-plex-sans` (400/500/600/700) and `@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono` (400/500), latin subset, self-hosted and imported once at the entry point — no Google Fonts CDN, so the app still works fully offline and makes no third-party request, which the local-only badge promises. `--font-sans` / `--font-mono` in `tokens.css` are now the only font stacks in the codebase, guarded by a unit test that scans the source. **New dependencies:** `@fontsource/ibm-plex-sans` and `@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono` — the self-hosting requirement rules out a CDN link, and these packages are just the woff2 files plus `@font-face` blocks (236KB of font files bundled, woff2 alone is about half that).
- [x] **Rename "Nested diagram" → "Nested Block"** — done 2026-08-26, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D50: the sidebar stencil, the box's own caption fallback, its badge tooltip, the ghost-link fallback and the right-click "Convert to nested block" action. Internal names (`NestedNode`, the `general/nested` stencil id, the model's `type: "nested"`) are unchanged — the rename is the user-facing noun only.
- [x] **Fix: two connections out of one nested block collapsed onto a single port** — done 2026-09-02, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D52. React Flow snaps a connection to any handle within its `connectionRadius`, so a second edge dropped near an existing port dot was handed that same port; inside the child both crossings then drew as ghosts at identical coordinates, so the earlier one was invisible and mapping "it" moved both. A port now only accepts a second edge if it is still free, and `splitSharedPorts` repairs documents already saved in that state on open.
- [x] **Onboarding 4 — export reminder toast** — done 2026-09-15, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D53: counts mutating actions since the last export by watching `doc` identity (one subscription, no per-action instrumentation), prompts at 15, re-arms at +40, two per session, and stays quiet during the untouched sample, the coach-mark tour, viewer mode and for 60s after an export. Counter persists under `BRAND.storagePrefix`; exporting resets it, dismissing does not. No model or `formatVersion` change.
- [x] **Ghost link ancestor chain**: a ghost link's hover tooltip now shows the full chain of connections back toward the root diagram (not just the immediate parent), and the pill is clickable to jump to the parent diagram — done 2026-08-14, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D41. PR #13.
- [x] **Nested-node connections always produce a port ("Option B")**: dropping an edge on a nested node's body auto-creates an unmapped port instead of a portless edge that vanished from inside the child; unmapped ports surface as amber "waiting to be linked" ghosts with a draggable loose endpoint, plus a banner, and three ways to resolve them — done 2026-08-20, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D42. Bumps `.deepdraw` to formatVersion 2. PR #14, stacked on #13 (shares the ghost-link code, so it merges after it).
- [x] **Nesting + canvas batch (7 items)** — done 2026-08-20, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D43: mapped ghost links now draw a dashed connector to the node they map to (the reported "links aren't coming" bug); draggable zoom slider alongside the 25%-grid +/- buttons; curved edges by default; drag either end of an existing edge to re-attach it; the sidebar's Nested-diagram entry carries the nesting accent; double-click renames a nested node (and its child diagram); and a nested node shows a live miniature of its child's contents instead of a stale "Empty". Changes three CLAUDE.md rules (collapsed view auto-generates, curved default, zoom slider). PR #15, stacked on #14.
- **Process incident (2026-08-20)**: PRs #14 and #15 showed MERGED but their work never reached `main` — both were *stacked*, so each merged into its parent **branch** rather than into `main`. #13 and #14 merged 10 seconds apart, so GitHub never retargeted #14's base from `feat/ghost-link-ancestor-chain` to `main` (and #15's from `feat/nested-auto-port-mapping`). Nothing was lost: `origin/feat/nested-auto-port-mapping` held the complete stack. Caught by the D35 `git merge-base --is-ancestor` check, recovered as PR #16. **Lesson: with stacked PRs, "MERGED" does not mean "on `main`" — always confirm the base branch, and let each PR's base retarget before merging the next.**
- [x] **Ghost-link interaction fixes + sidebar/Favorites polish** — done 2026-08-25, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D45: the loose endpoint attaches where it's aimed (it was drawn ~100px below the real cursor); ghosts attach to the side they're dropped on instead of always the left; mapped ghosts can be dragged to another shape or onto empty canvas to detach; the sidebar's Nested-diagram entry is no longer tinted like a selected item; stencils can be dragged into Favorites. Folded into PR #16 rather than a new stacked branch.
- [x] **Page size + PDF export/print** — done 2026-08-25, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D46: diagrams gain an optional `pageSize` (A4/Letter/Legal/A3/A5/Tabloid + orientation, or none for an infinite canvas), page boundaries render on canvas and tile once content spills, `File > Export as PDF` writes a real PDF, and `File > Print…` goes through the browser's own print pipeline. No new dependencies — the PDF is assembled by a hand-rolled writer (`file/pdfWriter.ts`), same approach as the existing PNG-chunk and SVG exporters.
- [x] **Fix: right-click context menu didn't open for a node in an active multi-selection** — done 2026-08-25, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D46. React Flow covers a multi-selection with its own overlay that swallowed the event; wired up its `onSelectionContextMenu` hook. Was sitting in the Inbox since D32.
- [x] **drawio XML import** — done 2026-09-15, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D55. `src/file/drawio/` reads `.drawio`/`.xml` (both the inline and the deflate-compressed `<diagram>` payloads, plus a bare `<mxGraphModel>`), mapping pages → diagrams, vertices → shapes, draw.io groups → `GroupNode`, and edges → `DEdge` with waypoints, routing, dash and arrowheads. Parent-relative geometry is resolved to diagram coordinates and every cell is re-issued a nanoid so two pages can't collide. **Best-effort is stated, not silent**: an unmappable shape becomes a correctly-placed, correctly-labelled rectangle and the import says which shapes it substituted; a half-attached edge is skipped and counted rather than fabricated onto a neighbour. The result goes through the same `validateDoc()` as any opened file. No `formatVersion` change and no new dependency — XML via `DOMParser`, decompression via the platform's `DecompressionStream("deflate-raw")`, the same reasoning as D51's gzip. Nesting has no draw.io equivalent, so an imported diagram is flat.
  - Also adds `src/file/openFile.ts`, one shared dispatcher now used by both File > Open and drag-and-drop, so a newly supported format can never work in one and silently not the other.

**Next up, agreed 2026-09-02** (user: "add into the queue ... export reminder toast, icon library sweep remaining slices, and drawio XML import"): these three run *before* the rest of Phase 3, in this order — Onboarding 4, then the icon library sweep (needs scoping first — it is currently a placeholder, not a plan), then drawio XML import. Each still ships on its own branch, one PR at a time, per CLAUDE.md.

#### Phase 3 — Portable diagrams — added 2026-09-02 from `docs/archive/portable-diagrams-spec.md`

Goal: a diagram that leaves the app shouldn't become a dead picture — wherever it lands (Word, PowerPoint, email, a wiki) the reader is one click from the real, drillable diagram. Everything here is **client-side only**: no diagram data is ever sent to a server, including ours, so the local-only badge stays literally true. Five items, in order, one branch and one open PR at a time per CLAUDE.md.

- [x] **Phase 3.1 — Diagram-in-URL codec + viewer mode** — done 2026-09-02, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D51: `src/share/urlCodec.ts` encodes a slimmed document to a URL **fragment** (never the query string — fragments are not sent to the server, which is the whole basis of the privacy claim); loading the app with `#d=` decodes, validates and renders a **read-only viewer** with working pan/zoom/nesting/breadcrumbs, a `Viewing a shared diagram · read-only` strip, and **Edit a copy** / **Download** buttons. Opening a share link must never touch the recipient's autosaved work. Size-classified at 8,000 / 16,000 chars; over the hard limit the link is refused and the file download offered instead. **No new dependency:** the spec proposed `pako`, but the app already requires the native `CompressionStream` (used by `file/pdfExport.ts` since D46), so gzip comes from the platform — see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D51.
- [x] **Phase 3.2 — Copy image with live link** — done 2026-09-15, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D56. `File ▸ Copy image with live link` renders the diagram at 2x on white with 24px padding and writes `image/png` **and** `text/html` (`<a href="{share url}"><img src="data:..."></a>`) as one `ClipboardItem`, so Word/PowerPoint/Excel/Outlook paste a hyperlinked image and everything else takes the plain PNG. The share URL's `&` is attribute-escaped — every link has one, between `#d=` and `&v=`, and a bare one is silently mangled by some HTML parsers. A document too large for a link **still gets its image copied**, with a notice offering the file download instead.
  - Also adds `src/app/Notice.tsx` + `ui.notice` in the store: a small dismissible feedback strip replacing `window.alert` for this flow. It carries an optional URL in a read-only field with a Copy button, which is what makes the "browser blocked the clipboard" path actually recoverable, and 3.3's Share dialog builds on the same surface. A notice with a link or a button never self-dismisses; a plain one expires after 6s.
- [x] **Phase 3.3 — Share link dialog** — done 2026-09-15, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D57. `File ▸ Share` shows the URL in a read-only field with a Copy button, a `Link size: x KB of 16 KB` indicator that turns amber past the warn threshold, the **Open at this level** checkbox (only offered when the sharer is actually inside a nested block), and the no-upload line. Over the hard limit the dialog refuses the link and points at `File ▸ Save` instead.
  - `&p=` carries a single diagram id, not a path: `model/nesting.ts`'s existing `findDiagramPath` reconstructs the whole breadcrumb trail from it. An id the delivered document doesn't contain falls back to page one rather than erroring — a stale or hand-edited link should still show the diagram.
- [x] **Phase 3.4 — PowerPoint export with native Slide Zoom** — done 2026-09-23, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D58. One slide per diagram depth-first, each nested block carrying a real OOXML Slide Zoom over its box targeting the child's slide, cover image replaced by the block's own collapsed view, Return-to-Zoom on, breadcrumb in the speaker notes.
  - **The prototype gate was run first, as instructed, and passed.** A two-slide deck with one zoom was built and validated before any of the exporter was written. The XML is built to the published `[MS-PPTX]` schema rather than guessed — two values would have been wrong from memory: `sldId` is the destination's `p:sldId/@id` (256, 257, …), not its rId or position; and a custom cover image is `imageType="cover"`, not `"custom"`.
  - **Both navigation modes ship**, chosen in the export dialog, rather than the link mode being a hidden fallback: Slide Zoom animates but can only be *created* on Windows and degrades to a still picture in older PowerPoint, while hyperlinked shapes work everywhere. The dialog states that limitation outright, per the spec's degradation note.
  - **No new dependencies.** The spec expected some; the ZIP writer is hand-rolled (~160 lines: CRC-32 plus two record layouts) over the platform's `CompressionStream`, the same reasoning as `file/pdfWriter.ts` in D46 and the gzip decision in D51.
  - **Not verifiable here:** "opens without repair prompts in PowerPoint" and the slideshow click-through. PowerPoint is not installed on this machine (checked). Every automated substitute is in place — Windows `Expand-Archive` extracts the package, all XML parses, all relationship targets resolve, an independent ZIP reader checks the parts — and the E2E writes both decks to `test-results/pptx/` so a person with PowerPoint can confirm in a minute.
  - [x] **L3 follow-up (tutorial spec, item L3)** — done 2026-09-23 as part of L1 (D63), not as a separate pass: 3.4 has merged and the export is confirmed working in PowerPoint (2026-09-23). Extend tour step 3's body with `Or use File ▸ Export to PowerPoint for slides that zoom into each Nested Block.`, add a secondary `Show me` button highlighting the menu item, and extend the L2 PNG tip to mention the PowerPoint export when the document contains a Nested Block. Keep step 3 to one card — past three short sentences, move the export options behind a "More ways to share" link rather than lengthening it.
- [x] **Phase 3.5 — Word export with diagram appendix** — done 2026-09-23, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D59. `File ▸ Export to Word` writes the first diagram as a captioned figure and every diagram below it as a numbered Heading-2 section in a **Diagram Appendix**, depth-first, so Word's own collapse and navigation pane work. Each nested block gets a transparent hyperlinked rectangle over it targeting that child's bookmark, plus a "Return to Figure N" link back. The whole `.deepdraw` JSON rides along in a custom XML part, and `File ▸ Open` reads it back — the docx is not a one-way trip.
  - **Navigation is doubled deliberately**: the overlay is the mechanism the spec asks for (Word has no image maps), but `a:hlinkClick` on a drawing resolves through a relationship rather than a plain anchor, so every figure *also* carries text links. The text form (`w:hyperlink/@w:anchor`) needs no relationship and cannot be dropped, which is what keeps navigation working in Word Online and in a Save-as-PDF.
  - **No new dependencies** — the spec expected some. The ZIP writer from D58 is reused as-is.
  - **Excel stays out of scope**, as the spec directs. Recorded here rather than left implicit: sheets-instead-of-headings would need its own package writer and buys nothing the Word appendix does not already give.
  - **Not verifiable here:** links resolving in Word desktop and Word Online, and surviving Save as PDF. Word is not installed on this machine. The package is validated structurally (Windows `Expand-Archive`, every XML part parses, every relationship target resolves) and the E2E writes the document to `test-results/docx/` for manual checking.
  - [x] **L3 follow-up (tutorial spec, item L3)** — done 2026-09-23 as part of L1 (D63): 3.5 has merged. Add the equivalent `Export to Word` line to tour step 3 and to the L2 tip, under the same one-card limit as the 3.4 follow-up. Do these two together if both are still outstanding; they edit the same card.

**Cross-cutting for Phase 3**: new code under `src/share/`, `src/export/pptx/`, `src/export/docx/`. **No `formatVersion` change** for 3.1-3.3, and 3.4/3.5 add no model fields either — if one turns out to need a field, stop and raise it rather than bumping the format silently. Every new user-facing string reads the product name from `BRAND`, and the onboarding copy rule applies throughout: never claim the data is "secure", "encrypted", or "private by design" — the accurate claim is that nothing is uploaded.

Whole remaining list moved into the active pipeline 2026-08-08 (user: "put them all in the pipeline and start working on them") — each still ships on its own branch, fully verified, one PR at a time, in the order above. Each branch is cut sequentially off `main` and doesn't see the others' not-yet-merged work, so decision-log numbering needs a renumber pass at whichever PR merges second, third, etc. (per CLAUDE.md's branch-workflow policy) — confirmed twice on 2026-08-10: once when PR #5 (Dark mode) merged before PR #3 (Minimap), and again when PR #3 merged before PR #4 (Find/replace) was resolved and pushed, re-triggering the same conflict on PR #4. All four PRs are now merged; policy changed the same day to one-PR-open-at-a-time going forward (see CLAUDE.md).

#### Phase 4 — Tutorial: live-link showcase — added 2026-09-23 from `docs/DeepDraw_Tutorial_LiveLink_Spec_20260923_v1.0.md`

Goal: the tour teaches drill-in and drill-out; it should also teach the second thing no competitor does — a diagram pasted into Word or PowerPoint stays clickable and reopens fully drillable. Taught twice: once in the tour, once at the moment the user is about to do the inferior thing. UI-layer only, no model or `formatVersion` change, and the onboarding copy rule applies throughout.

- [x] **L1 — Tour step 3, "Take it anywhere"** — done 2026-09-23, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D63.: extends the two-step coach-mark tour (D48) to three, spotlighting the closed **File** menu trigger rather than driving the dropdown. `Copy it now` runs the existing Copy image with live link on the sample (the click is the user gesture the Clipboard API needs), then the card becomes `Copied. Open Word or PowerPoint and paste to try it.` with a `Done` button; clipboard denial falls back to the D56 `Notice` with the URL. Introduces `{prefix}tourVersion` (current = 2) so a returning v1 user sees **only step 3**, once, prefixed `New:`, next time they are at the main level with at least one node.
- [x] **L2 — Moment-of-need tip on PNG export** — done 2026-09-23, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D64.: first time `File ▸ Export as PNG` runs (not the editable `.deepdraw.png`), let the export finish and then show a one-time tip on the `Notice` surface pointing at Copy image with live link, with a `Try it` button. `{prefix}liveLinkTipShown` makes it once-ever; never in viewer mode or during the tour.

#### Phase 5 — Templates — added 2026-09-23 from `docs/DeepDraw_Templates_Spec_20260923_v1.0.md`

Goal: nobody starts from a blank canvas. Wherever a diagram type is naturally hierarchical its template uses Nested Blocks, so templates double as the second place users meet the differentiator. Scope rule: expressible with shapes, edges, text, groups and Nested Blocks, with no data binding and no time-scaled axis.

**Hard rule across every batch**: a template may only reference stencils that exist in the library, are visible in the picker and are searchable. A shape a template needs but the library lacks gets added to the library *first*, with a label and `keywords`; same for edge features such as crow's-foot arrowheads. Enforced by a unit test, not by review.

- [x] **T1 — Infrastructure + starters** — done 2026-09-23, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D65. **Stencil audit found no gaps**: the three starters use only shapes that already exist in default-enabled sections, so no library work was needed.: the large PR. Sidebar **Shapes | Templates** switcher above the search box (last tab remembered under `BRAND.storagePrefix`), empty-canvas start panel with six featured thumbnails in a non-capturing layer, a `Start from a template` button on the first-visit sample strip, `File ▸ New from template…`, the template browser with collapsible categories and miniature-rendered thumbnails, the apply flow (direct into an empty page; `Add as new page` / `Replace this page` / `Cancel` otherwise, one undoable command, every id re-issued a fresh nanoid), the template format under `src/templates/` with lazy per-category loading, and the stencil-coverage test. Starters: Basic flowchart ★, Flowchart with subprocess ★⧉, Mind map ★. **Everything after T1 is mostly content.**
- [x] **T2 — Software & UML** — done 2026-09-24, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D66. **Stencil audit found real gaps and they were closed first**: three arrowheads (hollow triangle, filled and hollow diamond) did not exist anywhere in the app, the whole **C4 section** was missing, UML lacked activation bar / system boundary / node / artifact, and no UML stencil had search keywords. New `src/canvas/arrowheads.ts` is the single source of truth for arrowheads, shared by the canvas, the SVG export and the Style panel. Eight templates: class, sequence, use case, activity, state machine, component, deployment, and ★⧉ **C4 model** — Context → Container → Component as three real nesting levels, the flagship software template. **No new dependencies.**
- [x] **T3 — Data** — done 2026-09-24, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D68. **Stencil audit found four gaps and two things nothing else would have caught**: the five crow's-foot cardinality marks did not exist; there was no DFD section at all (Yourdon's data store and all three Gane & Sarson glyphs missing); there was no crow's-foot entity table; and all 10 entity-relation stencils had zero keywords. The two the audit caught: `arrowheads.test.ts` used `"crows-foot"` as its example of an *unknown* arrowhead, and `ArrowheadDef` structurally could not express a crow's-foot mark (one path, one paint — "zero or many" is a stroked fork plus an opaque ring), so it now carries `parts: {d, fill}[]`. New `dfd.ts` section (6 shapes, both dialects). Five templates: ER (Chen), ER (crow's foot), ★⧉ Levelled DFD (Yourdon/DeMarco), ⧉ Levelled DFD (Gane & Sarson), data pipeline. **Also fixed a canvas bug this batch exposed**: `
` in a label collapsed to a space, so every multi-line label — including T2's UML class compartments and C4 elements, already shipped — ran onto one line. **No new dependencies.**
- [x] **T4 — Architecture & Cloud** — done 2026-09-24, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D69. **Stencil audit found the icon sets sufficient but surfaced a renderer bug that would have wrecked the flagship**: `nesting/miniatureSvg.ts` drew every vendor icon as a blank tile, so an Azure landing zone's spoke thumbnails — the whole point of the template — were grids of empty rectangles. Fixed by nesting the raw vendor `<svg>` in a sized `<svg>`, the same re-anchoring ShapeNode and svgExport already use. Five templates: ★⧉ **Azure hub-and-spoke landing zone** (each spoke VNet a Nested Block, peering through a mapped port), network topology, AWS three-tier web app, GCP basic web app, ⧉ Kubernetes cluster (namespaces as Nested Blocks). **Kubernetes uses generic shapes and no vendor artwork**, per the spec's own condition — no openly licensed icon set is in the repo. **GCP's published icon set has no per-product Load Balancing, CDN or DNS icon** (we already ship it in full), so the front door uses the official networking *category* icon rather than something scraped. Featured is now exactly six, the spec's cap. **No new dependencies.**
- [x] **T5 — Business & Process** — done 2026-09-24, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D70. **Stencil audit found the largest gap of any batch**: no BPMN section at all (pool, lane, events, tasks, gateways, artifacts) and no swimlane containers, plus zero keywords on the whole flowchart section and 20 of 45 business stencils. New `bpmn.ts` (13 shapes) supplies both BPMN and the generic swimlane, since a swimlane *is* a pool with lanes. Eight templates: ⧉ org chart (each department a Nested Block), cross-functional process, BPMN (basic), fishbone, SWOT, business model canvas, customer journey map, milestone timeline. **Also fixed a canvas bug this batch exposed**: `RichLabel.align` was inert on the canvas — the label is a centring flex box, so `text-align` could not move a single-line label — while the SVG export honoured it. Third instance of canvas/export drift after D67 and D69. **No new dependencies.**
- [x] **T6 — Thinking & Planning** — done 2026-09-24, see CLAUDE_HISTORY.md D71. **Smallest audit gap of any batch, and the spec predicted it exactly**: the Style panel had whole-node `opacity` but no **fill opacity**, which is what a Venn needs — node opacity fades the outline and the label along with the fill. Added `fillOpacity` as a style key, wired through `StencilGlyph`, `ShapeNode`, `svgExport` and `miniatureSvg`, with its own Style panel slider so a user can draw a Venn by hand. Six templates: Venn two-set, Venn three-set, tree diagram, decision tree, concept map, sitemap. **No new dependencies.** **This closes Phase 5** — all six batches (T1–T6) done, **35 templates** across six categories (process 2, software 8, data 5, architecture 5, business 8, thinking 7), six of them featured.

**New stencil sections likely needed** (audit the library before each batch, add a section rather than scattering shapes): DFD, BPMN, C4, UML additions (lifeline, activation bar, fork/join, initial/final, system boundary), and swimlane pool/lane containers if groups cannot serve. Each follows the existing stencil architecture — lazy module, `StencilDef`, label plus `keywords`, hand-drawn original geometry, listed in `More Shapes…`.
